# Robert Babcock Family Tree (Ancestry.com)

**Tree**: "Robert Babcock Family Tree", tree ID 101867177. Root: Robert (b. 1945 father) / mother Cynthia Ann Rawson (1949–2002).
**Why:** Robert wants the most complete tree possible with photos + historical records, especially military service content.
**Standing requirement (2026-09-08, Robert):** every person on the tree should have a companion document capturing EVERY known detail about them — including unconfirmed/family-memory details — with clear labeling of what's confirmed (primary source or direct family confirmation) vs. unconfirmed (hearsay, hypothesis, single uncorroborated scrapbook mention, etc.). Build these as we go rather than after the fact.
**How to apply:** The original "direct ancestors only, skip siblings/aunts/uncles" scope (2026-08-30/31) was long ago superseded in practice — extensive collateral research is now core to the project (Curtis's siblings, Solomon's other children, Joshua Babcock's cousin line, Rawleigh's siblings Altha/Clarence/Hershel added 2026-09-07, the Keown/Looney/Phillips collateral work) whenever it helps solve a mystery, build a heritage paper, or follow up oral-history leads. Treat collateral relatives as in-scope by default now; the old restriction only matters as historical context for why early tree-building sessions were narrower. Still true and unchanged: no paid record purchases (skip Fold3/Newspapers.com paywalls, note them — though Robert since got newspapers.com All Access, see below); never use PersonMatch.aspx tree-merge hints (collapsed generations once — Silas married-to-his-own-mother incident); use direct merge URLs + manual flows, verify after saves.

## Workflows that work (Chrome MCP on ancestry.com)
- Merge URL: `https://www.ancestry.com/merge/tree/101867177/person/{pid}?sourceGid={rec}:{coll}&hid={hid}&xkcd=1&usePUBJs=true`
- Safe auto-include JS: check only "Include X" checkboxes whose context contains "in your tree" (matched people); manually check NEW-PERSON boxes ONLY for Father/Mother of a direct ancestor.
- Photo loop: hints page → Review links with mediaui-viewer → click Save button (exclude "someone else") → repeat with done-list.
- Potential-parent DNA cards: resize 1920x1080, click card button on facts page, click Yes in side panel (works at wide viewport).
- Hint extraction JS + collection IDs documented in session transcripts; 6482=WWI draft, 1002=WWII 1942 draft, 1666=Civil War draft 1863, 61861=VA Master Index, 8054=1850 census, 7163=1870, 6742=1880, 7602=1900, 7884=1910, 60525=FindAGrave.

## Marietta Blackwell orphan mystery (family lore CONFIRMED 2026-08-31)
Grandma Bernadine's story: Marietta Blackwell was an orphan, parents unknown, rumored Irish (possibly clergy). Robert confirmed the **Pfeffer family of Monroe Co IL adopted her**. Evidence found:
- 1880 census: "Mary Blackwell", age 3, **Oneida County Poorhouse, Rome NY**, listed ALONE, marked "Homeless", b. U.S., parents' birthplace columns blank (image 4243406-00127, coll 6742 rec 2318978).
- MO death cert #37226 (PDF: sos.mo.gov/images/archives/deathcerts/1938/1938_00037233.pdf): b. Feb 2 1877 New York; **father Unknown, mother Unknown**; d. Nov 1 1938 11pm St. Louis City Hospital (cerebral embolism); husband Jacob Brand; buried Columbia IL Nov 5.
- Married Jacob Brand 1904, Monroe Co IL, as "Marietta Blackwell" age 27 (her birth name). Obituaries say "nee Pfeffer" (adoptive family name). 1904 residence Utica NY (Oneida Co again) on timeline.
- Census parent-birthplace answers: 1920 "New York/New York", 1930 "United States/United States" → defaults; she didn't know. **No record says Ireland** — rumor stays oral-tradition-only (unfalsifiable; she didn't know her parents).
- Children: David (b.~1906, Robert's line), Marietta (b.~1911, m. Behnling), Laverne/Vern Harry (b.~1919).
- 1900 census gap: not found as Pfeffer/Marietta in Monroe Co — locating her 1880→1904 (Pfeffer household, possible orphan-train records: NY Foundling Hospital / Children's Aid Society) is the #1 next research goal. Oneida County Poorhouse admission records (NY State Archives) may name her parents!
- Caution: NY newspaper index item "Miss Mary Blackwell" (Rome Daily Sentinel 25 Nov 1910) merged to her profile is probably a DIFFERENT person (Vashti Mary Blackwell of Rome) — consider removing.
- Bernadine Brand (250177527837) fixed Living→Deceased (d. 28 Aug 2022 Portland OR) per Robert.

## ⭐ ANN BLACKWELL FOUND — Marietta's Irish mother (2026-08-31, session 2)
**Ireland connection CONFIRMED.** NY Census of Inmates in Almshouses & Poorhouses (Ancestry coll 1083):
- **1876 record** (1083/records/146687, image 31864_243932-00880, Oneida Co Poor House, Rec #446): Ann Blackwell, age 30, single, b. **Ireland**, house servant, admitted **30 Sep 1876** — 5 months before Mary's birth (Feb 2 1877). "Cause of Dependence: having young child." Relatives: None. Remarks: *"This is the second child this woman has had, the first one she swore on a boy by the name of Martin and he paid... about $20 in the hands of the County Superintendent as the child died some time ago. I do not know what can be done about this one as I understand she can not swear who is its father."* → Marietta's father: genuinely unrecorded/unknown. A child noted "in the House of the Good Shepherd" (Utica children's home — likely Mary's later placement).
- **1905 record** (1083/records/143812, image 31864_243935-00818, Oneida Co Almshouse, Rec #4115): Anna Blackwell, 52, single, last residence **Utica**, b. Ireland, **~40 yrs in U.S. (emigrated ~1865)**, occupation Domestic, religion **Catholic**, very feeble/sickness. **Father b. Ireland, Mother b. Ireland.** Relatives/Friends: **"Mrs. David Owens, 305 Sunset Ave., Utica NY"** ← traceable lead (possible sister?).
- TREE: **Ann Blackwell added as Marietta's mother, pid 252810842760** (abt 1846–, Ireland), both records attached. Robert's 3rd-great-grandmother.
- Next steps to Ireland: (1) trace Mrs. David Owens (1905 NY state census, Utica, 305 Sunset Ave); (2) Utica/Rome **Catholic parish registers** — Mary's Feb 1877 baptism (St. Peter's Rome or Utica parishes; Diocese of Syracuse archives) may name Ann's home parish; (3) Ann's death record (after Mar 1905, likely Oneida Co) — may name parents; (4) once parents' names known → rootsireland.ie Catholic parish registers; (5) House of the Good Shepherd Utica (still operating, hgs-utica.com) for Mary's placement file; (6) Oneida County History Center (Utica) for poorhouse admission ledgers beyond the state census forms.

## Owens-lead session results (2026-08-31, session 3 — from Robert's notes file)
Robert brought research notes (Downloads/ann-blackwell-owens-lead-research-notes.md, incl. "Scott's theory"). Findings:
- **Employer hypothesis CONFIRMED**: 1900 census (7602/records/49827834 & 49827839): David Owens (31, house painter) + wife Lucy at **305 Sunset Ave, Utica Ward 11** — household includes **"Annie Blackwell, 45, Servant, b. Jun 1854 Ireland, single, father b. Ireland, mother b. Ireland."** Mrs. David Owens = Lucy = employer's wife, not sister.
- **Paternity extension REFUTED for this Owens**: David Owens b. Nov 1868 — age 8 in 1876. Ann joined the household later; 1876 employer (if any) unknown.
- **HUGE: 1880 census, Rome (6742/records/3703106): "Ann Blackwell, 32, b. Ireland, Destitute" — SAME PAGE/dwelling as Mary (roll 903, p.61B, ED 112, dw 90). Mother and daughter were in the poorhouse TOGETHER in 1880.** Mary was never "alone" — index artifact. Paper's narrative should be updated eventually.
- Identity chain resolved: 1900 census she claimed "immigrated 1880, 0 children" = cover story (she was in Rome NY June 1880); only ONE Ann Blackwell in Oneida Co by 1900; 1905 form says prior almshouse inmate = Yes. Note: a second Irish Annie Blackwell (34, domestic, New Hartford) existed in 1880 — distinct person, keep in mind.
- **Ann's DEATH found: 31 Oct 1905, Rome NY (NY Death Index 61535/records/1125304), certificate #46049** — died in the almshouse ~7 months after admission. Death index attached to her tree profile along with 1880 + 1900 censuses.
- ⏭ NEXT: **order NYS death certificate #46049** (Rome city clerk or NYS DOH genealogy copy) — 1905 certs have father's name + mother's maiden name fields = best shot at Irish parents' names. Also: Utica city directories (manual download from uplinside.org), bastardy file for first child ("Martin", ~$20 via County Superintendent, Oneida County records), Mary's 1877 baptism (Syracuse diocese), Good Shepherd placement file, 1905 NY state census (Owens at 305 Sunset / Ann in Rome almshouse).
- **⭐ DEATH CERTIFICATE ORDER READY TO MAIL (2026-09-08).** Robert wants to find Ann's grave and physically visit it. Investigated: (1) Find A Grave + BillionGraves checked for a Blackwell memorial at both "St. Agnes"-named Utica cemeteries (IDs 65913 and 2660018) — no match at either, but BillionGraves shows only 160 cataloged/0 photographed records for that cemetery, so absence proves nothing (likely no permanent stone for a woman buried at charitable expense in 1905, or just unindexed). (2) St. Agnes's own local office (Anne, stagnescemeteryutica@gmail.com) already replied 2026-09-08: recent fire, no records/computer access until she's back in the office — genuine live lead, just delayed, no action needed until she writes back. (3) Found two independent routes that don't depend on St. Agnes at all: **Diocese of Syracuse Catholic Cemeteries central office, 2315 South Avenue, Syracuse NY, (315) 475-4639** (separate from the local Utica office; dioceses have been centralizing/digitizing cemetery records, may hold an unaffected copy); **Oneida County Historian's office, 1608 Genesee St., Utica, (315) 735-3642** (independent local archive, sometimes holds its own old cemetery/burial transcriptions). (4) **Actually pulled Rome NY's real genealogy-request process** (romenewyork.com/office-of-vital-statistics/ — NOT the certified-copy form, which explicitly bars genealogy requests and restricts certified copies to spouse/parent/child/sibling only; the correct one is "Application for Genealogical Services," which is open to any researcher once a NY death record is 50+ years old): **flat $22 fee per search** (fee table's "years" tiers are for how wide a date-range search is, not record age — since we have the EXACT date, it's the cheapest $22 tier), money order/certified check payable to "Rome City Clerk," photocopy of valid ID required, mail to Office of the City Clerk, 198 N. Washington St., Rome, NY 13440, 5-7 business day turnaround (a letter of request works just as well as their PDF form). **Built and delivered a ready-to-sign letter** covering every required field (name/date/place of death, parents unknown, no spouse, purpose, relationship as descendant) — files: "Ann Blackwell Death Record Request - Rome NY.docx/.pdf" in Desktop\Babcock Family History (build script scratchpad/build_ann_death_cert_letter.js). **Robert still needs to physically: get a $22 money order payable to Rome City Clerk, photocopy his driver's license, sign the letter, include a self-addressed stamped envelope, and mail all four to the Rome City Clerk address above.** This is the most likely single document to actually name the plot/undertaker and get Robert to the right spot in the cemetery, independent of the St. Agnes fire delay.

## Newspaper + institutional sweep results (2026-08-31, session 4 continued)
- **Ann's death notice found** (Rome Daily Sentinel 10/31/1905 + Utica Daily Press 11/1/1905): "Anna Blackwell aged 52, died in this city at 7 a.m. today from a complication of diseases. **The remains were taken to Utica, where she formerly resided, for interment**." → NOT potter's field; someone (almost certainly the Owens/Lucy) claimed her and buried her in Utica — likely **St. Agnes Cemetery** (Catholic; Lucy's sister's family Bach interred there). ⏭ St. Agnes cemetery records for Nov 1905 interment.
- **Owens household fully identified**: David E. Owens b. 11/15/1867 Utica (father John D. Owens, painter) — Utica FIREMAN from May 1896 (Truck/Engine No.1 Cooper St), d. 9/30/1925 at 1441 Sunset Ave. Married **Lucy (Ollerenshaw) Haganour** ~1900; house 305 Sunset was in LUCY's name; Lucy alive 1932 at 1443 Sunset (obit not found). Lucy's 1st husband Henry Haganour d. 1894 age 36. Barbara Haganour (b. Kirchen, Bavaria; Zion Lutheran; widow of mill laborer Henry Sr.) d. 1901 AT 305 Sunset, "lived with [Lucy] 24 years." Stepson Dr. Henry C. Haganour, dentist (Baltimore→Elkhart IN→Akron OH); Ada May Haganour m. Stephen P. Butler 1908 at **St. Patrick's (Catholic)**, reception at 305 Sunset; by 1925 Mrs. A.M. Butler of Oneida NY.
- **Children's Law of 1875** (NY Ch.173): children >2 barred from poorhouses → Mary's removal was STATE-MANDATED, not Ann's choice. Oneida Co practice (1877 SBC report): children → Utica asylums → "thence placed in families," county paid board. HGS = only Utica institution taking under-2s; Episcopal but took "many Roman Catholics."
- County Home cemetery (Floyd Ave, Rome, now MVCC campus): numbered 4" markers; plaque covers 1914–1931 only, no Blackwell — moot now, she's buried Utica.
- 1870s paternity/"Martin" search: clean negative in Fulton (OCR limits); Rome Semi-Weekly Citizen 1876-77 only on nyshistoricnewspapers.org (Cloudflare — needs human browser session).
- NYJA indentured ~7,000 NYC children into Illinois 1854–1906 (Kidder indexes: newyorkjuvenileasylum.com; Gridley IL orphan-train map) — checkable for Mary. NY Foundling "baby trains" = Catholic parish-priest channel. IL adoption statute 1867 → possible Monroe Co court record.
- Fulton History (fultonhistory.com) works for automated search where nyshistoricnewspapers is bot-blocked; family surname spelled "Haganour" in print.

## Family paper (2026-08-31; SECOND EDITION 2026-09-01)
"The Girl from the Poorhouse" — Marietta's full story written up for family. Web artifact (same URL both editions): https://claude.ai/code/artifact/3b299ec2-1b1b-495a-b501-0ebb53ac1052 ; PDF via reportlab script in session scratchpad; both editions **uploaded to Marietta's Ancestry gallery** (person 252810758012).
**2nd edition (10 pp.) adds/corrects:** 1880 "enumerated alone" corrected → mother & daughter together, separation was Children's Law of 1875; new chapters "Together, then taken", "Johanna Mary of Columbia" (adoption in print), "What became of Ann" (laundress → Owens 1900 cover-story census → death 10/31/1905 → remains taken to Utica by friends; includes embedded women's-day-room photo), "The girl on the ship" (Scotland manifest, held as *probable*), "The road back to Ireland" (Louth/Dunleer–Limerick–Clare map; Clare Ennis Catholic-Blackwell clergy echo of Bernadine's rumor, framed cautiously); paternity "can not swear" given both honest readings; coda updated ("crossed alone and was carried home by friends"). First-edition PDF still in gallery (not deleted — Robert can remove via Ancestry if he wants only the 2nd).
**Revision 2026-09-01 (Robert pushback, IMPORTANT STANDARD):** Robert challenged "the Owens employed her" — correct: only evidence of employment is the single 1900 census word "Servant"; 1905 form says "friends"; Lucy's household demonstrably took people in (Barbara Haganour 24 yrs; stepdaughter's 1908 wedding reception at 305 Sunset; no other record of Owens keeping a servant). Paper revised: "employer's wife"/"family she served" removed; new caution paragraph presents servant-vs-taken-in openly and lets the reader weigh it; lineage line now "buried in Utica by the friends who took her in". **Robert's standing rule for family papers: nothing made up; where a reading is logical but unclear, present the evidence and let the reader interpret — label interpretation as interpretation.** Revised PDF re-uploaded to Marietta's gallery (title "...Second Edition, revised"); the earlier unrevised 2nd-ed upload retitled "SUPERSEDED draft — safe to delete" (deletion left to Robert).
**REFINEMENT (2026-09-08, Robert, after the Ann-in-Utica theory episode — see the "GIRL FROM THE POORHOUSE 4th EDITION" entry below): "It's ok to infer things that make sense but it just needs to be clear that it's speculation and the logic behind the theory."** This isn't a walk-back of the no-fabrication rule — it's the other half of it: don't just flag something as a guess and stop there (too timid), and don't present a theory without showing your work (too opaque). The bar is: state the inference, label it clearly as inference/theory, AND spell out the actual reasoning chain that makes it plausible, so the reader can independently judge whether the logic holds. Applies to every paper going forward, alongside [[feedback-family-papers-style]] (no myth-debunking sections) — these two rules together are basically Robert's full editorial standard for family history writing now.

## Immigration + Ireland-origins session (2026-08-31, session 5)
**Passenger-list candidate (LEADING, not proven):** Ann Blackwell, age 15, spinster, steerage, ship **Scotland**, Liverpool → New York, arrived **28 May 1866** (Ancestry coll 7488 rec 5628049, image NYM237_266-0161). Math: 28 May 1866 → Mar 1905 admission = 38.8 yrs ≈ her "about 40 years in U.S."; age 15 → b. ~1851 (midpoint of her 1846–1854 age spread). Traveling ALONE (between Finnegan family of 7 and unrelated laborers). **Caveat:** nativity column reads "English" on her own line — BUT same clerk ditto'd "Bryan Hart" (Irish name) as English too; column unreliable. Liverpool was the standard Irish route (Drogheda/Dublin ferry → Liverpool), esp. for the Louth cluster. **Uniqueness: she is the ONLY Ann/Annie/phonetic Blackwell female arriving in the U.S. 1861–1869, any port, any Ancestry immigration collection** (9 total hits; rest = males/Australia/1 masked Canada record behind World Explorer paywall — coll 1263 rec 7261199, check free on FamilySearch). Record ATTACHED to Ann's card per Robert (2026-08-31): Departure (Liverpool) + Arrival (28 May 1866 NY) facts merged; Birth "abt 1851" deliberately NOT merged (kept tree's abt 1846 Ireland). Her timeline now also shows the old "Arrival 1880" fact from the 1900 census cover story — both visible; flagged to Robert.
**Ireland Blackwell map (agent report, full text in tasks/a0dd6dfd2e3e84f8e.output):** Blackwell rare in Ireland (152 bearers 1901) and **60.5% Catholic** (92 RC / 54 CoI). Griffith's Valuation ≈57 households, three clusters: (1) **Co. Louth — Dunleer 7 hh** (+Ardee/Tallanstown; densest single parish; Dunleer RC baptisms survive from 29 Oct 1847; Louth emigrants sailed via Liverpool ✓); (2) **Co. Limerick 16 hh** — Tuogh/Cappamore-Murroe 5 + Limerick city; (3) **Co. Clare 10 hh** — Ennistymon/Kilshanny (documented Catholic Blackwell lineage in Ennis: James Bartholomew Blackwell b.1763 — note the CLERGY-connections echo of Bernadine's lore); also Roscrea Tipp/Offaly cluster (mixed religion). Watch confusable surname **Blackall**.
**Search strategy for Robert (free-first):** ① 1901 census religion filter at census.nationalarchives.ie → re-rank counties; ② FamilySearch (free acct) "Ireland, Catholic Parish Registers 1740-1900": Ann/Anne/Anna/Nancy Blackwell/Blackwall/Blackall bapt. 1844–1856, then read images at registers.nli.ie (browse-only, free); ③ Dunleer register page-turn 1847–56 if index thin; ④ irishgenealogy.ie free church search (Dublin/Kerry/Cork only — quick elimination); ⑤ US-side shortcut stays king: death cert #46049 + St. Peter's/St. Mary's Rome parish burial record + St. Agnes Utica plot may name parents/county directly. St. Peter's Rome (est. 1837, Irish parish) = almost certainly Ann's parish; free burial transcription 1837–1882 at freepages.rootsweb (check for earlier Blackwells — a pre-1876 relative death would explain "Relatives: None"). Civil birth reg. only from 1864 (no help); no Irish outbound lists exist for 1860s.

Also untried: HathiTrust's copy of the same book (Cloudflare-blocked to the research agent; a logged-in human could get through).
- **HONEST BOTTOM LINE, restated stronger**: after checking every sibling's death record/obituary reachable tonight, a completely independent and unusually well-sourced parallel tree, and multiple published county histories — NO ONE anywhere has produced a document with the literal words "Curtis, son of Solomon Babcock" (or Ross/Ira/Anson, for that matter). What exists instead is an extraordinarily dense, zero-contradiction convergence: 5+ independent sources (1903 book, our tree, heidir999/Heidi Murray, Gayle Trautman, two published county histories) all give the identical set of names/birthdates/birthplaces for Solomon's children, all place multiple of them in the same few square miles of Stephenson Co IL within the same years, and Peleg's actual will (primary, now triple-corroborated) proves Solomon himself is real and exactly where/when claimed. This is precisely what the **Genealogical Proof Standard** is designed for: reasonably exhaustive search + accurate source citation + analysis/correlation of conflicting/consistent evidence + soundly written conclusion — it does NOT require one single "smoking gun" document when the correlation is this dense and nothing contradicts it. Recommend the paper explicitly invoke GPS-language for this one link rather than either overclaiming ("proven") or underselling ("just a family tradition").

## 🚨 ROUND 5 SAME NIGHT (Robert: "leave no stone unturned") — MAJOR NEW BIOGRAPHICAL DISCOVERY about Curtis himself + a 4th independent tree, but the Solomon link itself still not directly document-proven
- **⭐⭐⭐ CURTIS BABCOCK WAS DECLARED INSANE AND JAILED AS A PAUPER, Jan 1858, Freeport, Illinois — completely new, previously unknown fact.** Found via a THIRD independent researcher's tree (Gayle Trautman, Ancestry tree 40253138, person 19469923787 = her Curtis Babcock). Two Freeport Journal clippings (7 Jan 1858 "CURTIS BABCOCK.—The Board of Supervisors have appointed Dr. Mattis, Jas. Millikan and F. Baker a committee to take charge of Curtis Babcock, the insane man now confined in the County Jail. They have full power to act..."; 14 Jan 1858, a formal county-board Resolution referring "the case of Curtis Babcock, now confined in the County Jail as an insane pauper" to a supervisory committee, auditing jailer Wm. W. Smith's bill for "keeping said Babcock"). **Neither clipping names any family member** — just county officials. This explains a lot: no obituary, no probate, no clean death record for Curtis anywhere — he most likely died indigent/institutionalized.
- **Gayle Trautman's Curtis Babcock page also has**: an actual 1848 marriage record image (Cortland Co. Clerk's transcribed marriage book) — "October 3—Curtiss Babcock, age 26 yrs. of Scott, and R[?]landa [surname illegible/cut off in the transcription], age 18, both of Scott, by Rev. A. C. Whiting" — confirms Curtis's own age/marriage/residence but the bride's surname is NOT legible in this specific scan, so **"Randall" vs "Goodman" remains genuinely unresolved** (her tree labels the linked person "Loranda Randall" but the source image itself doesn't clearly show that surname). A 1850 census "Curtis Babcock... Occupation Farmer... Listed with wife, Lorenda, and son James Anson" — 1850 Age 26, Scott, Cortland NY (i.e. Curtis had NOT yet moved to Illinois as of July 1850 — new precision on the migration timeline). His death is bracketed by her research as **"Aft. 3 Jul 1865 and Bef. 28 Jul 1870"** (last positive record = 1865 IL State Census, alone in Oneco household; gone by 1870) — i.e. no actual death record exists, it's a computed absence-bracket.
- **A FOURTH independent tree found** (Nancy Woltersek, Ancestry tree 40078443, person 19447009622): gives Curtis's wife as yet a THIRD surname variant — **"Lorenda JACKSON"** (so we now have three candidate maiden names in circulation: Randall [1903 book + Gayle], Goodman [1881 WI marriage record, James Anson's own testimony], Jackson [this 4th tree] — genuinely unresolved, though Goodman has the strongest claim since it comes from a primary record naming James Anson's own parents, even if it might be a stepfather's surname rather than her birth name). This 4th tree also independently states Curtis **died 1870, Galva, Henry Co., Illinois** — the first time a specific year+place death claim (not just a computed bracket) has surfaced; matches our own tree's pre-existing (previously unsourced) "Death: Galva, Henry, Illinois" fact.
- **Checked BOTH the New York (coll. 8800) and Illinois (coll. 9048) Ancestry probate/will collections directly for "Curtis Babcock" around 1870 — zero hits in either.** No estate, no will, no administration anywhere for him. Combined with the insanity/pauper finding, this is a coherent, sad picture: he likely died with no estate to probate, possibly still confined, which is exactly the profile that leaves no paper trail for us to find.

NY "Curtis Babcock" probate entry (turned out to be a woman, unrelated Babeck family); a Chicago Tribune "Curtis & Babcock" hit (an unrelated 1860 business partnership name, not a person); an 1888 Freeport civic-officer list mistagged to Curtis in the 4th tree (illegible/irrelevant).

## 🎯 ROUND 5 PAYOFF — Jacksonville State Hospital admission record FOUND (asylum-records agent). Resolves Curtis's 1858 fate, reveals a NEW sibling, doesn't yet name Solomon but gives a concrete next actionable step for Robert personally.
- **THE RECORD**: *Directory of Jacksonville State Hospital Patients 1854-1870* (transcribed by genealogist Shirley Aleguas directly from the hospital's own admission ledger; underlies a 2019 peer-reviewed study). Archived (RootsWeb page is dead) at: **https://web.archive.org/web/20241006040255/http://sites.rootsweb.com/~ilmaga/morgan2/statehosp/mc-sh_adm-b.html**
  > **Babcock, Curtiss | Age 36 | NY | Admitted 26 Jan 1858 | Discharged 21 Jul 1858 | Co. of Res.: Stephenson | Nearest Kin/Guardian/Notes: "Brother of Mrs. Davis of that county"**
- **Timeline now fully resolved**: 7 Jan 1858 Freeport Journal notice → 14 Jan 1858 county board resolution appointing a committee → **26 Jan 1858 transferred to Jacksonville State Hospital** → **discharged alive 21 Jul 1858** (NOT died there — checked the companion deceased-patients list, he's not on it) → returned to Stephenson Co., matching him being alive/alone in the Jul 1865 IL state census. Age 36 in Jan 1858 = b. ~1821-22, consistent with known birth window.
- **⭐ NEW SIBLING DISCOVERED**: "Brother of Mrs. Davis of that county" — a previously totally unknown SISTER of Curtis (and of James Anson's aunt), married to a man surnamed Davis, resident in Stephenson Co. by 1858. Not found in genealogytrails' free Stephenson Co. marriage index — the marriage likely happened in Cortland Co. NY before the family's migration. **Worth a targeted Cortland Co. NY marriage-record search for "Babcock-Davis."** Does NOT name Solomon directly, but is itself real, substantive new genealogy — another of Solomon's children to add to the tree once confirmed.
- **The record type that WOULD have parents: "Commitment papers, 1851-1943" (Illinois State Archives, Record Series 252.003)** — explicitly includes "parent information (names, birthplaces...)" per its own finding aid. **BUT these are legally closed records** — per a 2019 peer-reviewed study on this exact hospital, access requires being a close relative or having a genealogical/family-health reason, "a court order... may well be necessary." **ACTIONABLE FOR ROBERT: call Illinois State Archives at 217-782-2647, cite descent from Curtis Babcock (admitted 26 Jan 1858, discharged 21 Jul 1858, Stephenson Co.), and request the Commitment Papers for that admission date** — the series is chronologically arranged so the record is locatable by date even with no name index. This is now a real, concrete lead beyond what web research alone can finish.
- Also identified but requiring in-person/mail access: **Stephenson Co. Probate Claim Dockets 1848-1859 and 1864-1865** (IRAD-Northern Illinois University, 815-753-1807) — might hold a guardianship entry, though unlikely for a pauper with no estate. **No Board of Supervisors minutes survive for 1850-1869 at all** — meaning the two Freeport Journal clippings we already have may be the ONLY surviving record of that county board action.
- Ruled out: Elgin State Hospital 1880 census (not there); Find a Grave/BillionGraves (no match); Illinois Digital Newspaper Collections (Cloudflare-blocked to the agent — a human could try manually at idnc.library.illinois.edu).

Always type into all three fields, take one throwaway screenshot to confirm the values stuck, and only then click the search icon; if a submit clears a field, redo it once more before trusting the result count. The in-viewer page-number jump box is also unreliable for multi-page browsing — prefer opening exact hits from search results instead of trying to page through an issue by number. Panning within a page image works via `left_click_drag` (not scroll wheel) once at least one "+" zoom level has been applied. The `zoom` tool's region coordinates have repeatedly captured stale/wrong content on this site — prefer the page's own "+" zoom controls plus a plain screenshot instead. **To get an actual saveable image of an article: the `computer` tool's `save_to_disk` option does NOT reliably save to a locatable path on this machine — instead use the newspapers.com page's own "Print/Download" button → "Entire Page" → "Save as JPG", which downloads a real full-page JPG straight to `C:\Users\Robert\Downloads\` (confirmed working) — then crop the relevant article out locally with Python/PIL (`Image.open(...).crop((x0,y0,x1,y1))`, proportional coordinates based on where the article sits on the page work fine) before sending it to Robert.**

A separate "Brothers and Sisters" divider page shows a portrait of 5 people captioned "Clarence - Hershel - Rawleigh / Gertrude - Altha" — matches a photo of 3 older men + 2 older women, i.e. the 5 surviving siblings (Lillie already deceased by then) photographed together later in life.

**Rawleigh Oliver Babcock — death circumstances, primary-source death certificate [DOC] transcribed in full:** Certificate of Death, State of California, Cert# 388100 04394, Certification T-89-1772003. Rawleigh Oliver Babcock, male, white, b. 30 Jan 1903 Illinois, d. **23 Nov 1988**, age 85, widowed, usual occupation "Plumbing Contractor," self-employed, residence 3405 N. Palm, Fresno; father "Arthur Babcock, Illinois," mother "Emma Brisby, Illinois" (⚠ note: certificate says Emma was born in Illinois — conflicts with the established Tennessee birthplace from Emma's own scrapbook page and the tree; death-certificate birthplace fields for a parent are frequently informant-guesswork and are the LEAST reliable field on the form, so this does not override the Tennessee sourcing, but flagging the conflict for completeness). **Place of death: Fresno Community Hospital. Informant: Stanley Babcock (son), 1714 W. Fedora, Fresno.** Cause of death chain (handwritten, partly hard to read): cardiopulmonary arrest ← sepsis ← "Organic Brain Syndrome." Certifying physician Mohinder Poonia M.D. Burial 28 Nov 1988, Fresno Memorial Gardens, Lisle Funeral Home. **The scrapbook itself, in Bernadine's hand, states plainly: "Dad Died November 23, 1988 from an overdose of Drugs given him in a Nursing Home"** — paired with a clipped LA Times article "Nursing homes overdrug elderly — study" (Janny Scott, on Harvard research into chemical-restraint overmedication in nursing homes) as the family's own explanation for how "Organic Brain Syndrome"/sepsis arose. **This is the family's belief about a difficult death, presented alongside (not necessarily contradicting) the official certificate's clinical cause chain — treat as documented family testimony, not a settled medical finding, if this goes on any card.** Pallbearers at Rawleigh's funeral: **Bob Babcock, Bruce Babcock, Ed Babcock, Gerald Babcock, Ivey Horne, Hugh Moffett** — "Ed Babcock" is a third previously-unknown likely-cousin name (alongside Bruce and Gerald from Emma's 1959 funeral) — probably sons of Hershel or Clarence Babcock, unconfirmed; Horne and Moffett are new surnames, likely in-laws, also unconfirmed. Rawleigh and Mary Ella share a joint bronze grave marker at Fresno Memorial Gardens reading "BABCOCK."

**Mary Ella (Chapman) Babcock ("Ella"/"Mom") — full vitals and siblings, previously very thin in the tree:** **b. 20 Mar 1900 Kansas, d. 3 Jul 1983 (scrapbook's own handwritten note says "July 5, 1983" — one-of these-two-dates discrepancy, both sourced from the same scrapbook, unresolved) Fresno, with Alzheimer's disease.** Obituary ("Mary E. Babcock," Fresno paper): graveside services Fresno Memorial Gardens, lived in Dinuba 27 years before moving to Fresno, housewife, member United Christian Church; survived by husband Rawleigh; son Stanley Babcock of Fresno; **two sisters: Eunice [Chapman, surname possibly mis-set as "Babcock" in the clipping — needs a clean read] of Salinas, and Ethel Limberg of Monrovia** (note: Monrovia CA — same town where Rawleigh's daughter Rosalie worked in the WWII defense plant, a nice geographic echo); three grandchildren; four great-grandchildren at time of death. Married Rawleigh 2 Apr 1921 (their 50th-anniversary "Golden Wedding Day" scrapbook page, a surprise party at Stanley's home, confirms the date exactly: "Rawleigh and Ella were very surprised. April 2, 1971"). A page titled "About our Mom": loving, kind, wonderful cook, always had a full table when Stanley and Rosalie were growing up, won first prize 3 years running at the Tulare County Fair for her "Sour Cream Raisin Pie," also did sewing/knitting/crocheting/tatting. **Caption note: "Mom's complete life is in the Chapman Albumn" — implies a SEPARATE Chapman-family scrapbook/album exists somewhere (not yet seen) — worth asking Robert/Sandi if it can be found, since it would presumably cover Mary Ella's own Chapman ancestry.**

**Rawleigh's other biographical detail, all new:** Held CA hunting license from 1917/1918 (scrapbook: "Rawleigh's first Hunting License," dated "17-24-18" — likely a garbled read of 1917 or 1918), 5'11", blue eyes. Avid bowler — "bowled until he was over 80 years old, won many trophies." Volunteer fireman in Dinuba for many years ("kept his boots and turnouts by his bed, ready to get up at any hour to answer the alarm"). Enthusiastic gardener at the Palm Ave. house — Fresno Bee clipping "FALL HARVEST" photographs him picking tomatoes from a volunteer vine that grew nearly 13 feet tall. Served two terms on the Dinuba City Council. Baptized (with Mary Ella) at First Baptist Church, Fresno (Rev. Bernie G. Osterhouse), 15 Dec 1955 — fellow baptism-class names printed alongside them, if ever useful: Betty Elaine Boone, Mr. & Mrs. Milton A. Edwards, Roy Clements Haley, Mr. & Mrs. Chester Hamilton, Clifford George, Mr. & Mrs. Robert E. Hirsh, Judy Barbara Jolley. Social Security card issued 28 Jan 1939 (SSN 545-26-1727); Medicare hospital insurance effective 1-1-68, medical insurance 7-1-68.

- **PDF versions of BOTH documents also built and delivered (2026-09-06/07)**, using a NEW reusable technique since soffice/pandoc are unavailable on this machine: unzip the docx, convert `word/document.xml` (+ embedded `word/media/*` images via base64 `<img>` tags, + `_rels/document.xml.rels` for image lookups) to a styled standalone HTML file with a small custom Python script (`docx_to_html.py` in scratchpad — handles headings/bullets/tables/quotes/callouts/images by walking the XML tree), then `"C:\Program Files\Google\Chrome\Application\chrome.exe" --headless=new --disable-gpu --user-data-dir=<scratch>/chrome_pdf_profile --print-to-pdf=<out>.pdf --no-pdf-header-footer --run-all-compositor-stages-before-draw --virtual-time-budget=20000 file:///<path-to-html>`. Verify with `pymupdf` (render pages to PNG, `Read` them to visually check) since no other PDF viewer/renderer is on this machine. **This exact pipeline was reused successfully for the Illinois Archives letter PDF and for "The Girl from the Poorhouse" 4th edition PDF (built straight from its Artifact HTML, no docx involved) — it's now the standard method for any docx-or-HTML→PDF need on this machine.**

- **Environment note for future docx builds**: soffice.exe and pandoc are BOTH absent on this Windows machine — verify generated .docx files by unzipping + parsing `word/document.xml` with Python `xml.dom.minidom` (catches malformed XML) plus targeted content greps. This caught a real bug: docx-js's `Document` children array silently mis-serializes a nested array (from a helper like `callout()` returning multiple Paragraphs) as a literal `<0/>` XML tag instead of throwing — always spread multi-paragraph helper results into `children.push(...)`, never `children.push(helperReturningArray(...))`.

- **Technique note**: this Ancestry.com session's browser viewport is 2880px wide but `computer` screenshots render at 1568px — raw pixel-coordinate clicks on the Add-Family modal are unreliable (they land on the wrong element). Workaround that worked reliably: use `javascript_tool` to find elements by exact `textContent` match and `.click()` them directly (bypassing coordinates entirely), and to set React-controlled `<input>` values via the native `HTMLInputElement.prototype.value` setter + dispatched `input`/`change` events. Search-result-list links (virtualized) sometimes don't respond to either `computer` click-by-ref or coordinate click — for those, read the `href` via `read_page` and `navigate` directly instead of clicking.

## ⭐ "GIRL FROM THE POORHOUSE" 4th EDITION SHIPPED (2026-09-07) — the orphan train chapter
Robert asked to get as much detail as possible on the orphan-train claim he and Dad/Aunt Sandi kept coming back to for Marietta, and to explain what orphan trains were in the paper itself. Same artifact URL (favicon 🕯️, republished label "fourth-edition-orphan-train"): https://claude.ai/code/artifact/3b299ec2-1b1b-495a-b501-0ebb53ac1052 — local copy of the full HTML at C:\Users\Robert\.claude\projects\C--Users-Robert-Desktop-RedMServer\0a652eab-6523-437b-8475-24e9e1e2afe1\tool-results\artifact-3b299ec2-1788390963-fbc2.html (this is now the canonical source file to edit for future editions — read it before publishing again). PDF (20pp) built via headless Chrome print-to-pdf (same technique as the Babcock heritage paper — `chrome.exe --headless=new --print-to-pdf` on the artifact's saved HTML) and sent to Robert; no reportlab script this round.
**Four new chapters inserted between "Together, then taken" and "Johanna Mary of Columbia, Illinois":**
- **"What an orphan train was"**: general history — Charles Loring Brace's Children's Aid Society (founded 1854), 150,000–200,000 children moved 1854–1929, the reasoning (get children out of institutions into working farm families) — explicitly tied to the same 1875 NY Children's Law already in the paper that took Mary from Ann.
- **"Which train, and whose family"**: the two strongest candidate placing agencies, presented honestly as unresolved — (1) **New York Foundling Hospital** (Sisters of Charity, opened 1869, ran Catholic "baby trains" 1875–1914 specifically for infants/toddlers, parish-priest-vetted families, numbered tags + matching cards, 3-page indenture signed at the station) — fits Marietta's Catholic destination family; (2) **New York Juvenile Asylum** (from 1855, formalized by special Illinois legislative act in 1861, sent children "almost exclusively" to Illinois for 41 years, indentures required schooling/clothing/church attendance + $75 terminal payment for girls at 18) — fits the Illinois destination better than any other candidate. **Checked newyorkjuvenileasylum.com's own partial name index for "Blackwell" — 6 entries (Albert, Harold E., Harrold E., James b.1912, Robert, William), none female, none matching — an honest negative, not conclusive** (list is admittedly incomplete/hard-to-read handwriting per the site's own disclaimer).
- **"The promise about school"**: Grandma's specific new memory (relayed by Robert 2026-09-07) — "one of the terms of the adoption was that Marietta would be educated, but it never happened" — presented as a serious, checkable claim, cross-referenced against real historical fact that shorted schooling was the most commonly broken orphan-train indenture term. **Countervailing evidence found**: 1900 census (record 7602/15411605, "Jennie Pfeffer") shows **Can Read: Y, Can Write: Y, Can Speak English: Y** at age 24 — doesn't disprove Grandma's memory (literacy ≠ full formal schooling) but means it wasn't total illiteracy either. **Key honest gap**: the 1890 census that would have caught her actual school-age years was destroyed by the 1921 fire — genuinely unrecoverable, not a research failure.
- **"The doctor, the brother, and the theater"**: addresses Aunt Sandi's exact testimony (already in memory line ~218) point by point. **"St. Louis doctor" CLARIFIED by Robert (2026-09-07): Sandi meant Pfeffer himself**, not a separate person — paper corrected accordingly (removed the earlier draft's speculative "maybe an unfound intermediate physician placement" line): presented now simply as family memory upgrading a prosperous small-town merchant into "a St. Louis doctor" over three-four generations of retelling ("the profession changes, the man does not"). The "brother" ties to the already-flagged undocumented-sibling lead, now explained via the documented historical practice of orphan-train agencies routinely separating siblings to different towns; the theater/reunion-lecture story matches a real, well-attested phenomenon (orphan-train alumni touring to publicly search for lost siblings, per the National Orphan Train Complex, Concordia KS) — specific East St. Louis/Columbia theater event and Joseph Brand's "contractor built a theater" claim NOT yet found/reconciled (Joseph is documented elsewhere only as a house carpenter — flagged open, not debunked). **Robert's Ann-in-Utica theory — CORRECTED after Robert pushed back (2026-09-07):** first draft wrongly argued the timeline was against Ann having been in Utica during Mary's Good Shepherd years (reasoning: Ann's first documented Utica record is the 1889 city directory, after Mary was likely already placed in Illinois). Robert correctly pointed out this was backwards — the paper had ALREADY established both that Mary went to an institution IN UTICA and that Ann later built her whole life IN UTICA, which is exactly what supports (not undermines) the theory that Ann followed her daughter there and stayed close until the orphan train took her away. Fixed: absence of an Ann directory listing before 1889 is absence of evidence, not evidence of absence (directories miss transient servants/laundresses); now presented as the paper's own best explanation for a fact it never otherwise explained — why Ann settled and stayed in Utica specifically, of everywhere in NY she could have gone. **Lesson for future editions/papers: when the user proposes a theory, check first whether existing established facts in the paper actually support it before drafting a rebuttal — don't manufacture a timeline argument from an absence-of-record gap.**
- **New/renumbered lead in "The leads we are following"**: added lead #6, "The two placing agencies" — direct research request to NY Foundling Hospital records (held at NYU Special Collections + Sisters of Charity of NY) and/or the NYJA researcher's private ledgers, naming Marietta's age + mid-1880s window, as the most promising untried step (also the best remaining shot at finding the brother). Old Ireland leads 6–7 renumbered to 7–8.
**Intro paragraph updated** to introduce the 4th edition's addition; eyebrow line bumped Third→Fourth Edition.

## ⭐⭐ CURTIS BABCOCK → SOLOMON BABCOCK PARENTAGE — DEEP RESEARCH (2026-09-08, in progress)
Robert's explicit top priority: "the biggest thing we need solved." Starting point: the father-son link between Curtis Babcock (1824–, pid 252812663612) and Solomon Babcock (1776–1867, pid 252812663769) rested ENTIRELY on the 1903 "Babcock Genealogy" book (p.164, compiled/secondary source), with ZERO primary sources attached to either profile. Robert is separately calling Illinois today re: the related Curtis-in-asylum thread (Jacksonville State Hospital, IL, 1858 — from earlier session research).
**Still no single document explicitly states "Curtis, son of Solomon" outside the 1903 book** — but the circumstantial case is now much stronger via multiple independent, mutually-consistent primary records:
- **1824 Scott, NY school census** (Justice of the Peace book, Town of Scott, Cortland Co., 1816-1837, District No. 2 — FamilySearch via Ancestry hint, submitted by researcher "heidir999"): lists "Babcock, Solomon — 5" (5 children ages 5-15 sent to school) in **1824, the exact year and township of Curtis's recorded birth**. Confirms Solomon was an established head of household there that year. Same page lists other Babcock households nearby — Ezra, Maxon, Paul Jr., Rubin, Justice, Silas — likely Solomon's brothers/relatives (Maxon = Solomon's documented brother Maxson, see Find A Grave sibling list below).
- **1840 US Census, Homer, Cortland NY** (Solomon's household, 6 people total, ages only — pre-1850 censuses don't name members): Solomon (60-69, matches b.1776), wife (50-59, matches Amy Anna Morgan b.1783), a female 20-29 (unidentified, possibly unmarried older daughter), **TWO males 15-19** (consistent with Curtis b.1824→age 16 AND Austin P. b.~1825→age 15) and **ONE male 10-14** (a still-unidentified younger son). Circumstantial but consistent.
- **1855 NY State Census** (Solomon's own record): explicitly lists **"Austin P Babcock," b. abt 1825, Father Soloman Babcock, Mother Anna C Babcock** — a previously-undocumented SON of Solomon, same birth-year range as Curtis, with a genuine primary-source parent-child link (just not for Curtis himself). NOT yet added to tree.
- **Marriage record transcription** ("Census, 1850-1860, and vital records, 1847-1849, 1860, of the town of Scott, Cortland County, N.Y.," transcribed from the original Cortland Co. Clerk's Office records, submitted by "sislesait"): "**October 3—Curtiss Babcock, age 20 yrs. and R[.]landa[?], age 18, both of Scott, by Rev. A. L. Whiting of Scott**" — dated the document itself "January 30, 1849, Martin K. Knapp, Town Clerk." Confirms Curtis's marriage to Loranda [Goodman] on 3 Oct 1848 in Scott, Cortland NY with an exact date (already in tree, no source attached — now documented). Note: age 20 in 1848 implies b.~1828, not 1824 as currently recorded — a minor discrepancy worth flagging, common in period records. Does NOT name parents. **Same document/page also lists "May 17—Charles Kinyon...and Hulda Babcock, age 23 yrs. of Scott" — a likely previously-undocumented DAUGHTER of Solomon (b.~1825, same generation as Curtis/Austin P.), NOT yet in the tree.**
- **1850 US Census**: Curtis's own household, Scott, Cortland NY — himself (27, b.abt1823 NY, farmer), wife Loranda, son Anson J (matches James Anson Babcock 1849-1932 already in tree). Confirms independent household by 1850; doesn't bear on parentage.
- **Baptism catalogue** (Baptist Church, Homer NY, Elder Alfred Bennett, "Miscellaneous records of Homer NY 1794-1860," FamilySearch via heidir999): records Solomon's OWN baptism 25 July 1807 alongside wife Amy Morgan, plus "Jared Babcock" and "Susan Clapp Babcock" baptized same day (likely another relative pair) — predates Curtis by 17 years, family/religious color only.
- **Find A Grave hint** (not yet reviewed/saved) adds: **Mother "Ruth Babcock"** (Peleg's wife, previously undocumented) and a previously-undocumented child **"Luke P Babcock"** — the same "Luke P Babcock" also appears independently in the **1865 NY State Census** as a child of Solomon. Two independent sources agreeing — worth adding to tree.
- **U.S. Family History Books hint** (the 1903 book, now also surfaced as a formal Ancestry hint) lists child "**Ralph Babcock**" rather than Curtis — possibly a mis-transcription/OCR of "Curtis," or a genuinely separate, previously-unknown child. Not yet resolved — worth checking the original book page image.
- Peleg W. Babcock's other children (Solomon's siblings, via Find A Grave): Lucy, Benjamin, Tabor, Maxson, John Babcock — Maxson matches the "Maxon Babcock move to Cortland Co. NY" history-book excerpts (from "Pioneer History; or Cortland County and the border wars of New York" by Hermon Camp Goodwin) also sitting as unreviewed hints on Solomon's profile.

## 🔎 ANN BLACKWELL DEEP-DIVE — OVERNIGHT AUTONOMOUS SESSION (2026-09-10)
Per Robert's explicit fallback instruction ("dive deep on the newly discovered possible parents of Ann Blackwell... go as far back on her line as possible... especially see if you can find her story anywhere. We will need enough information to have a headstone created"). Ann Blackwell pid = 252810842760.
**NEW FACTS FOUND** (all already sourced/attached in the tree, previously unreviewed):
- **Immigration record identified**: arrived NY 28 May 1866 aboard the ship **"Scotland,"** departed Liverpool (NARA microfilm M237, NY Customs Passenger Lists). She traveled ALONE — no family listed with her on this record. One indexed version of this record gives her birth as "abt 1851" rather than "abt 1846" — a discrepancy worth noting, would make her a teenager (~15) at immigration rather than ~20.
- **TWO separate New York Poorhouse/Almshouse census entries** already sourced on her card: one dated 30 Sep 1876 (Oneida), consistent with the 1880 census also showing her (with Marietta) in the Rome, Oneida NY poorhouse.
- **1900 census**: by then she was NOT in the poorhouse — working as a **servant** in a household in Utica Ward 11, Oneida Co, still single/unmarried.
- **No FindAGrave memorial exists for her** (checked 2026-09-10) — confirms the "120 years forgotten, no headstone" framing is accurate as of today, not just per the cemetery's own reply.
- **General Ancestry search for Irish parish/civil records naming a "Patrick and Catherine Blackwell" with daughter Ann b.~1846 Ireland found NOTHING usable** — Irish civil birth registration didn't start until 1864 (predates her by ~18 years), so anything earlier is parish-register-only and needs a specific county to search, which we don't have yet.
**TECHNIQUE NOTE — Ancestry's image viewer resisted automation for zooming scanned records**: repeatedly tried to zoom into the 1876 poorhouse census image and the 1866 ship manifest (magnifying-glass toolbar buttons, scroll-wheel, double-click, keyboard +/-, direct image src extraction via JS) — every attempt either did nothing or accidentally triggered browser-back navigation back to the search results page. The toolbar zoom-in icon at roughly (10,50) in the viewer's coordinate space appears to overlap with a "back" hit-target that fires instead. **Workaround that DID work**: use Ancestry's general search (`ancestry.com/search?name=...&birth=...` etc., or click through from a source citation's "View" link) to reach the image viewer, then read the INDEXED summary fields shown on the search-results card itself (Name/Birth/Death/Residence/Departure/Arrival) — these are reliable and don't require reading the actual scanned image at all. Only fall back to the scanned image (and accept a low-res `zoom` crop, since no working zoom-in was found) when the indexed fields are insufficient. If a future session needs the actual poorhouse ledger detail (which might name a "next of kin" or origin not captured in the index), it may be worth trying a fresh page load or a completely different navigation path (e.g. arriving at the image viewer via the family-tree person page's own "Explore" links per-fact, not yet tried since those turned out to link to generic decade/location stock photos instead) — or just asking Robert to view it himself, since human-eyes-on-scan may simply be more reliable here than any automation path tried so far.
**DELIVERABLE SENT**: `C:\Users\Robert\Desktop\Babcock Family History\Ann Blackwell - Life and Headstone Reference.docx` via SendUserFile — a narrative write-up of her life plus a clean "for the headstone" block (name/birth/death/burial location) and a "what's still open" list (pending Rome NY death-cert mail reply from 2026-09-07, no Irish county yet, poorhouse ledger detail not fully extracted, no FindAGrave memorial created yet).
**Still open / good next steps for a future session**: (1) check whether the Rome NY vital-records mail reply has arrived; (2) if Robert can view the 1876 poorhouse ledger image himself (or a future session finds a working zoom path), look for a recorded "nativity"/parish/next-of-kin field; (3) consider creating a FindAGrave memorial for her (not done — public-facing action, hold for Robert's explicit go-ahead per this project's normal practice); (4) Marietta's father is unnamed in every record found so far — worth a dedicated search pass if this becomes a priority.
**Follow-up pass, same night**: found a SECOND poorhouse census entry, dated 16 Mar 1905 (Oneida) — confirms she was back in the almshouse only ~7 months before her death, not just as a young mother in the 1870s-80s. This record gives yet another birth-year variant ("abt 1853"), on top of 1846/1848/1851 seen elsewhere — logged in her Research Notes as "treat 1846 as best estimate" given the spread. Checked Newspapers.com via general search: no obituary found for her (unsurprising for an indigent almshouse death). Research Notes on her card now reflect all of this. This closes out what's practically achievable without the pending mail reply or a human reading the actual ledger scan.
**Ship images found (Robert's request, next morning)**: identified the vessel — the iron steamship **SS Scotland**, National Line, built 1865 on the Tyne (builder John Scott). Found two public-domain images on Wikimedia Commons: a period painting of the ship under sail/steam ("Steamship Scotland 1865.jpg", source tynebuiltships.co.uk) and a Harper's Weekly engraving of her wreck ("Wreck of the steamship 'Scotland' off Sandy Hook, 1866.PNG", 29 Dec 1866 issue, sketched by C. Parsons). Dramatic detail: the Scotland collided with the American ship Kate Dyer and wrecked off Sandy Hook, NJ just ~7 months after Ann's May 1866 crossing — total loss, though everyone aboard both ships survived. Both images downloaded to `C:\Users\Robert\Desktop\Babcock Family History\` (filenames: "SS Scotland (1865) - the ship Ann Blackwell sailed on.jpg" and "SS Scotland wreck - Harpers Weekly Dec 1866.png") and delivered via SendUserFile. Ship-fate detail also folded into her Ancestry Research Notes. **Note**: could not upload these images directly into Ancestry's own gallery for her profile — the browser-automation toolset here has no reliable way to drive a native OS file-picker dialog for a `<input type="file">` upload; if a future session wants them attached as actual Ancestry media (not just Research Notes text), that likely needs either a different tool/capability or Robert doing the upload himself from the two files already saved to disk.

## 🔎 CURTIS'S WIFE'S NAME — THREE-WAY VARIANT CONFLICT SURFACED, GAP STILL NOT CLOSED (2026-09-09)
Robert photographed the actual 1903 Babcock Genealogy page (p.164, entry 292 "Deacon Solomon...Children:...Curtis, b. 1824; d. Galva, Ill.; m. Dorana Randall") and separately the actual 1848 Cortland Co. Clerk marriage-record transcription page ("October 3—Curtiss Babcock, age 25 yrs. and R.Ianda?, age 15 both of Scott by Rev. A. L. Whiting of Scott. ... Dated, January 30, 1849, Martin E. Knapp, Town Clerk."). Verified the book excerpt against the digitized full text at archive.org (`babcockgenealogy01babc_djvu.txt`, line ~15768) — matches exactly, confirming it's genuinely printed that way, not a misread; searched the same volume for "Loranda"/"Goodman" and found zero other occurrences, meaning the book's compiler never had that name in their own records at all.
**Net result: THREE non-matching names for the same woman**, and the gap this project has flagged since [[genealogy-babcock-tree]]'s "CURTIS BABCOCK → SOLOMON BABCOCK PARENTAGE" entry above is **not closed by this** — if anything it's more caution-flagged than before:
1. **"Loranda Goodman"** — the tree's prior working name (source of the "Goodman" surname specifically is unclear/unverified — the 1848 transcription itself, the one primary record for this marriage, does NOT give a surname at all).
2. **"Dorana Randall"** — per the 1903 Babcock Genealogy, p.164, entry 292 (secondary, family-compiled, 55 yrs after the fact).
3. **"R. Ianda ?"** — per the actual 1848 Cortland Co. Clerk marriage transcription (the one genuine primary source), where even the ORIGINAL transcriber marked the bride's name uncertain with their own "?", and gave no surname. Groom's own name is spelled "Curtiss" (double-s) in this document. Bride's age 15 in Oct 1848 → b. ~1832-33, consistent with the tree's existing "Loranda Goodman b.1832" on the birth year alone.
**Fixed**: rewrote Research Notes on both Curtis Babcock (pid 252812663612) and Solomon Babcock (pid 252812663769) to document all three variants explicitly, cite the 1903 book precisely (p.164, entry 292, exact quoted text) instead of a generic reference, and flag the book's reliability on this specific branch given its name doesn't independently corroborate anything else known. Did NOT rename the "Loranda Goodman" person record on the tree itself — no variant is confirmed correct, so the existing placeholder name stays, with the ambiguity fully captured in the notes instead of a false-confidence rename.
**Still open**: whose surname is actually correct is unresolved; the Solomon→Curtis parentage link still rests on the 1903 book ALONE (same status as before, just now precisely cited) — this did not provide new corroborating primary evidence for the parentage claim itself, only for the marriage event's date/place/groom's age.

## ⚠ CURTIS BABCOCK NEWSPAPERS.COM SWEEP — GENUINE COVERAGE GAPS FOUND (2026-09-08)
Per Robert's "go through newspapers.com again" request: searched for Solomon's 1867 obituary (Homer/Cortland Co. NY) and Curtis's obituary/death notice (Galva, Henry Co. IL). **Result: newspapers.com has NO digitized local paper for Cortland County NY at all** (searching "Cortland" or "Homer" in the Newspapers filter returns only Nebraska/Kansas/Alaska/Michigan/Louisiana towns of the same name, zero NY hits) — confirmed via control test: The Post-Standard (Syracuse, the nearest digitized NY paper) has ZERO indexed pages for the entire decade 1860-1870 (a "died" test search came back empty), so its coverage simply starts later than Solomon's 1867 death. This is a genuine archive gap, not a search-technique failure (control searches for common words also returned zero in the same window, confirming absence rather than a UI bug).
For Henry County, IL (where Curtis reportedly died in Galva): coverage DOES exist from ~1865 onward (Henry County Republic, Kewanee Daily Courier, Kewanee Star, Henry County Chronicle/Cambridge). "Curtis Babcock" (quoted) returns zero hits 1855-1885 in Henry Co. specifically. **One unexpected find**: The Henry County Chronicle (Cambridge, IL), Jan 10 & Feb 21 1865, carries a legal notice — "STATE OF ILLINOIS, HENRY COUNTY... Elizabeth Babcock vs. M. Purdy, John Purdy, Adaline [Purdy], Rome Babcock, Josephine Babcock, Ellen E. Babcock, Alfred Babcock, Arthur Babcock, Ida Babcock, and William McMurtry" — a chancery suit, Elizabeth Babcock as plaintiff, bill of complaint filed 3 Jan 1865. **These names (Rome, Josephine, Ellen E., Alfred, Arthur, Ida) do NOT match any of Curtis's known children (James Anson, an unnamed daughter later Mrs. S.W. Hutchison, Henry) — flagged as a likely UNRELATED Henry County Babcock family, not pursued further, but worth a second look if the Curtis connection ever needs Henry Co. context.**
**⏭ Not yet tried**: Illinois probate/court records for Curtis's actual estate in Henry or Stephenson Co.; FamilySearch (rather than newspapers.com) for a Cortland Co. NY paper, since newspapers.com simply doesn't have one; Cortland Co. NY probate/will index for Solomon (never attempted).

This is NOT a random stranger — "Ted" was apparently what pen-pal/extended-family correspondents called Stanley.
2. **Letter from Stanley & Bernadine Babcock to "Dear Doris"** (undated, but references answering "4-30-06"): explains how "Ted Babcock" (=Stanley) first made contact — their **son Robert ("Bob") was buying/selling old Corvettes online**; a stranger named Ted Babcock (Stanley, using that nickname) reached out about Corvettes and, seeing the shared surname, sent Bob genealogy info asking if they might be related. Bob passed it to his parents Stanley & Bernadine, who were in contact with a "Royce Babcock" in Illinois; they forwarded it to Royce but never heard back. **This is presumably the origin of exactly this genealogy research thread** — Stanley/Bernadine reaching out to distant same-surname Babcocks to see if there was a real connection. ## ⭐⭐⭐⭐ BREAKTHROUGH (2026-09-08): the "Ted Babcock" Illinois connection is REAL — Stanley Babcock's own great-grandfather was James (Anson) Babcock
Going through the rest of the "genuinely new" photo batch from Aunt Sandi (a family-history workbook/scrapbook, evidently made by Stanley and/or Bernadine, containing hand-drawn charts, photos, and actual primary documents) turned up the confirming evidence:
- **A hand-illustrated family tree ("family tree of Stanley Ross," self = Stanley Ross Babcock)** lists his ancestors including, on the Babcock paternal side: father **Rawleigh Oliver Babcock**, grandfather **Arthur Babcock (1875-1922)**, grandmother **Emma Mae Brisby**, and — critically — **great-grandfather "Anson Babcock"** married to **great-grandmother "Olive Picket [Pickett]."**
- This matches, almost certainly, the **"James Anson Babcock"** documented in the Illinois "Keen-Babcock Family Tree" (see the entry above) as the son of Curtis Babcock and father of William Byron Babcock (b. 1871, Oneco IL) — and that same Illinois document explicitly states **"Arthur Babcock — Brother to Wm. Byron — settled in Salinas, California area."** Arthur's birth year (1875) lines up almost exactly with James Anson's documented marriage to Adelia Leah Pickett ("m. 1875" per the Illinois doc) — "Olive Pickett" in Stanley's chart is very likely the same wife, remembered under a different first name/nickname a few generations later (Adelia Leah vs. "Olive" — a real discrepancy still worth chasing down, but the surname match plus the timing match is compelling).
- **Independent confirmation found in the same photo batch**: a 1945 clipping from the *Salinas Californian* ("2-26-45") announcing the marriage of **"S2/c and Mrs. Marvin Bruce Babcock"** — the groom is "**Marvin Bruce Babcock**, son of Mr. and Mrs. **Clarence Babcock, Salinas**" (bride: Alta May Maple/Strauss). This is almost certainly the **"Bruce Babcock"** from the family's own email answers this session (son of Clarence and Eunice Babcock, married to "Alta," later Harvey Cummins's wife) — and it independently places **Clarence Babcock (Rawleigh's brother, already in the tree) living in Salinas, CA**, exactly the town the Illinois letter names for the "Arthur Babcock" branch. Two unrelated documents (an Illinois family's own compiled genealogy, and a 1945 California newspaper clipping from Bernie/Stanley's own scrapbook) now converge on the same fact: **this family has a real, documented branch in Salinas, CA, tracing to Arthur Babcock — exactly what Doris Schulz's 2006 letter asked about.**
- **Conclusion**: The chain **Curtis Babcock (1824–) → James Anson Babcock (1849–1932) → Arthur Babcock (1875–1922) → Rawleigh Oliver Babcock (1903–1988) → Stanley Ross Babcock (1921–2013) → Bob → Robert** is now a strong, well-corroborated hypothesis, not just a guess — but it still rests on Stanley's own hand-drawn chart (a family memory document, not a primary record) plus circumstantial-but-convergent evidence. **This is exactly what Robert wants to verify next with primary sources**: census records placing Arthur Babcock as a child in James Anson Babcock's household, a marriage record for James Anson + Adelia Leah/Olive Pickett, birth record or church record for Arthur Babcock in Illinois ca. 1875, etc.
- ⚠ One loose end: Robert says he's never heard his grandfather Stanley called "Ted," and thinks the original 2006 correspondent (whoever first wrote to "Bob" about Corvettes) was simply confused about the name — so "Ted Babcock" may not really be a Stanley nickname at all, just a mix-up by a stranger who'd never met him. Doesn't change the genealogy finding above, which stands on its own regardless of who "Ted" was.

### Lillie Babcock's branch — previously almost entirely undocumented, now resolved
Lillie Babcock married a man surnamed **Brown** (per an actual funeral-home "Family Record" intake form for Emma Sindlinger — CONTRADICTS the family email's claim "married a Mr. Williams"; ⚠ unconfirmed which is correct, flag both). Lillie died young; children (per the scrapbook narrative, presumably reliable since written by a direct family member): **Doris** (oldest, m. **Marion Filmore/Fillmore**, 3 children: **Donald, Gail, Jimmie** Filmore), **Darlene** (m. **Worley Weaver** per the scrapbook narrative page — but the ORIGINAL funeral bearer lists for both Emma Sindlinger's and Mary Ella's funerals spell this pallbearer's name **"Worley Wheeler,"** not Weaver — ⚠ unconfirmed/conflicting: may be one person with an inconsistently-recorded surname, or two different men who happen to share a first name; Darlene's husband and the pallbearer "Worley" may not even be the same person), and **Dennis** (died as an infant of diphtheria). Doris and Darlene were raised by "Grandma Sindlinger" (Emma) after Lillie's early death. **Donald Filmore** (the pallbearer from Robert's original open questions) = confirmed as Doris's son, Lillie's grandson.

### Hershel Babcock's branch — fully resolved
Hershel Babcock married **Mavis Barrows** (60+ year marriage; Mavis had a twin brother; Mavis d. 2 Dec 1997 age ~90, funeral at Dopkins Fresno, pallbearers Harold Newton Jr., Stanley Babcock, Matt White, Leonard Barrows, Donald Fillmore, Rich White — "Barrows"/"Newton" bearers suggest these are Mavis's own relatives). Three children: **Gerald**, **Virginia** (became engaged, died the day after her engagement party, unmarried, no other details), **Dean** (Hershel's own son — died age 18 of a bad heart; NOT the same "Dean" as Gerald's later son, a namesake). **Gerald Arthur Babcock** married **Donna Johnston** first (d. 13 Nov 1969, age 40) — two children **Dean A. Babcock** (b. 18 Mar 1949, d. 7 Oct 2008 age 59, m. Betty; predeceased by father Gerald; survived by sister Darla Anton) and **Darla Babcock** (m. **Michael Anton** — real 1970s Fresno newspaper wedding announcement found: bride's parents "the Gerald Babcocks," groom son of Mr./Mrs. Albert Anton; groom's cousin ushers Dennis Contrestano/Pete Moosoolian; Dean Babcock, "brother of the bride," was an usher). Gerald remarried **Betty Fischer** 31 May 1970, no children together. Gerald enlisted in the Navy 1944 (real clipping: son of "Mr. and Mrs. H.O. Babcock" [Hershel O.?], trained Farragut ID → Camp Parks → Port Hueneme Seabees, sent overseas late Sept. 1944).

### Clarence Babcock's branch (the "double cousins") — fully resolved
**Clarence A. Babcock married Eunice Chapman** (Mary Ella Chapman Babcock's own sister — making their children Rawleigh's "double cousins" via both a Babcock-brother and Chapman-sister connection). Clarence served in the WWII Seabees, was in Samoa Sept. 1943, hospitalized in San Diego with tropical fever, convalescent home in Santa Cruz (real newspaper clipping); the family lived in **Salinas, CA** — independently corroborated by a 1945 Salinas Californian wedding clipping for their son. **Three sons**: 
- **Robert Harold "Bob" Babcock** — married **Patsy M. (Parton)** — she was a food-service supervisor for Salinas City Schools 28 years (real Salinas Californian feature article); Patsy b. 18 Sep 1925 Selma CA, d. 27 Feb 2001 Salinas (real obituary); **Bob (Robert Harold) predeceased her, d. 1996** (per Patsy's obituary — a concrete death date not previously known). Bob served in the Army, entered Feb. 1943, trained Camp Shelby MS, stationed Fort Benning GA (real WWII clipping). Children: **Susan Diane** (b. 9 Jan 1957 Salinas, m. name Escobar — husband unclear, kids Natalie & Mindy Escobar, plus a granddaughter "Krisie Babcock" via son David) and **David** (b. 11 Mar, one daughter Krisie). NOTE: this "Bob/Robert Babcock" (Robert Harold, Clarence's son) is a completely different person from **Robert Stanley "Bob" Babcock** (Stanley's own son, Robert's father) — same nickname, different men, same generation — keep clearly distinguished on the tree.
- **Bruce Babcock** (full name **Marvin Bruce Babcock** per a 1945 wedding clipping) — married **Alta Maple** 6 Feb 1945; three children **Sharon, Bill(ie)/William, Rick(ie)/Richard** (a "Tina" also appears grouped with Alta/Sherry/Bill/Rick in a floral-tribute signature — unclear if a 4th child or an in-law, unconfirmed). Bruce & Alta divorced; Bruce remarried **Norma**; Alta later married **Harvey Cummins** (Rawleigh's son-in-law, widower of Rosalie) — she was in the room when Harvey shot himself (per Bob/dad's email, not yet in any written document found).
- **Ed(die) Babcock** — first wife **Donna**, three sons **Michael, Randy, Stephen**; Donna died from an illegal abortion after an affair, while the boys were very young (per family email; independently, a floral-tribute list confirms "Ed, Carol Babcock & boys," i.e. Eddie's household by the time of a later funeral). Altha Ethelyne Babcock (youngest of Arthur+Emma's 6 kids) married **Edgar "Ed" Shipley**, a rancher who grew grapes on a ranch in **Munson, CA** (between Dinuba and Visalia). ## ✅ LOJUANDA/JERRY RESOLVED AS NICKNAME, NOT NAME CONFLICT (2026-09-09) + CHART MERGE-LINE FIX + SCHLOTTACH GENERATIONS ADDED TO VISUAL
Robert: "I'm sure Jerry or Jerri is a nickname. Don't change her name but put the nickname in quotes." Resolved the long-flagged Wallace Weaver wife-name item this way (not as a rename, as a nickname annotation): edited her Ancestry "Quick Edit" name field (reached via Edit ▾ → Quick Edit in the top toolbar, NOT a fact-row edit — this person had no Name fact row visible in the timeline since she has no birth/death recorded) from "LoJuanda" to **LoJuanda "Jerry"**, and rewrote Research Notes on both her page (252813120791) and Wallace Weaver's page (252812968124) to describe it as a nickname carried alongside the funeral-register name, not a disputed/conflicting identity. Technique note: reaching Quick Edit reliably needed a JS click on the actual `<button class="link icon iconEdit">` element (`computer`-tool clicks on the visible dropdown item didn't register), then the standard native-setter `<input>` trick to set the name field.

Robert also caught two problems with the "James to Sandi" org-chart (2026-09-09 build): (1) the maternal connector visually ran from **David Brand's own box** into Stanley Ross Babcock's box — wrong, since David is Bernadine's *father*, not Bernadine herself; (2) the Schlottach generations (documented in the tree, see [[genealogy-babcock-tree]] main data above) weren't in the visual at all, only named in the legend text.
**Fixed** by restructuring the chart's merge logic: introduced a reusable "marriage tie" pattern — two boxes at the same row, joined by a short horizontal line with a small dot marker, where ONE side (the one whose own descendants are drawn) carries the vertical drop to children, and the OTHER side is a dead-end tributary. Applied it twice: Estella "Stella" Schlottach (now her own box, descending properly from Robert C. Schlottach + Clara Jane Sewell, who descend from Ferdinand Heinrich Schlottach + Albertine Kottwitz) ties into David Brand's box (David's own box now solo, descending from Marietta J. Blackwell + Jacob Emil Brand); their child Bernadine Brand (her own box now, not just text inside Stanley's box) ties into Stanley Ross Babcock's box, whose own line continues down to Bob & Sandi. This is the general pattern to reuse for any future "two ancestries merging by marriage" situation in a box-and-connector chart: never let one spouse's own ancestor chain terminate by connecting directly into the *other* spouse's parent-generation box — always land it on that spouse's OWN box, one generation removed from the in-law.
Final canonical file (same paths, redeployed in place): `C:\Users\Robert\Desktop\Babcock Family History\james-to-sandi-orgchart.html` + `.png`. Now 41 boxes / ~45+ people, still one page. `build-tree.mjs` (the Node layout script) lives in the session scratchpad, not the project folder — if a future session needs to edit this chart again, it will need to be rewritten from this description rather than reopened from disk.

## 🚨 CORRECTION: family tree charts must be real org charts (boxes + connector lines), not styled card/document layouts (2026-09-09)
Robert's feedback on the first "James to Sandi" build (card-grid + vertical ladder-list style, CSS/HTML document aesthetic): "This looks nothing like a tree and nothing like the image i gave you. Cant you make it look more like an org chart would look in visio?" He wants an actual hierarchical diagram: rectangular boxes per person/couple, connected by elbow/right-angle lines, laid out generation-by-generation top-to-bottom with siblings fanning out left-to-right under a shared bus line — the classic Visio/PowerPoint org-chart and genealogy-software look, NOT a styled prose document with cards, ladders, or bulleted lists standing in for "a tree."
**Fixed** by writing a small Node.js layout script (`build-tree.mjs`, kept in the session scratchpad) that computes real tree geometry programmatically: recursive subtree-width algorithm (leaf width = box width; parent width = max(box width, sum of children's subtree widths + gaps)), then top-down center-x assignment, then renders plain SVG `<rect>` boxes + `<text>` labels + polyline/elbow connectors (vertical stub → horizontal bus → vertical stub into each child, matching real org-chart connector style) — no card grids, no CSS list styling standing in for tree structure. A side chain (e.g. the Blackwell/Brand maternal line) that merges into the main descent tree rather than descending from it is positioned as its own small vertical stack and joined in with a polyline elbow + arrowhead marker into the target box, not folded into an inset text box.
**Lesson for all future genealogy chart requests**: default to this real box-and-connector SVG org-chart approach from the start, not a styled document/card layout — even though the latter looks "designed," it does not read as a tree to someone expecting a genealogy chart. Verify by eye that the output has visible connector lines running between literal boxes before rendering to PNG.
Final file: `C:\Users\Robert\Desktop\Babcock Family History\james-to-sandi-orgchart.html` + `.png` (rendered 4380×2140, headless Chrome light mode) — supersedes the card-style `james-to-sandi.html`/`.png` as the canonical chart. Same data scope as before (James Babcock 1612 → Bob & Sandi, 12 generations, Blackwell/Brand maternal tributary, 6 collateral sibling branches), minus a couple of generic "child"/"child" placeholder boxes for Wallace Weaver's two unnamed children (removed — better to leave him as a leaf than show meaningless placeholder nodes in a finished chart).
