## ✅ FULL-NAME CORRECTION PASS COMPLETE (2026-09-09) — Robert: "I intend to share this family tree with everyone in the world," so no relative/informal references
Robert flagged that Research Notes facts were using relative/informal terms ("Dad," "Sandi," "Bernadine's scrapbook," "Robert's own dad") that only make sense to him personally — inappropriate for a tree meant to be shared publicly and used by future researchers. **Standing naming convention adopted for ALL Research Notes going forward:**
- "Dad"/"Bob" (when referring to Robert Stanley Babcock, Robert's father, rsbabcock@sbcglobal.net) → always write out **Robert Stanley Babcock**
- "Sandi" (when referring to Robert's aunt) → always write out **Sandra Kay Babcock Walsberg** (maiden name Babcock; m.1 Munday, m.2 Walsberg — "Walsberg" is her current married surname)
- "Bernadine's scrapbook"/"Bernie's scrapbook" → **Bernadine Brand Babcock's scrapbook**
- Also confirmed: Robert wants the tree to "contain every bit of context a future researcher will need to recover their lost histories and the personal stories behind them" — keep full narrative richness (WWII service detail, family lore, etc.), the fix is naming only, not trimming content.
**Went back and corrected all 45 of the ~48 Research Notes facts already created** (using a live in-place "Edit fact" + JS find-replace-on-textarea-value + Save script, much faster than recreating facts — this Edit-in-place approach DOES work reliably via JS `Save` button click, contradicting an earlier same-day note that in-place editing was unreliable; that earlier finding was specific to `computer`-tool clicks on Save, not JS clicks). Un-touched because they never had the issue: James Anson Babcock, Adelia O Pickett, Curtis Babcock, Solomon Babcock.
**Reusable technique for any future correction pass** (find-and-replace across many already-saved Research Notes facts without retyping content):
```js
async function fixSourcesGeneric(replacements) { // replacements: [[oldStr,newStr], ...]
  function setVal(el, value) {
    const proto = el.tagName === 'TEXTAREA' ? window.HTMLTextAreaElement.prototype : window.HTMLInputElement.prototype;
    const setter = Object.getOwnPropertyDescriptor(proto, 'value').set;
    setter.call(el, value);
    el.dispatchEvent(new Event('input', {bubbles:true}));
    el.dispatchEvent(new Event('change', {bubbles:true}));
  }
  const heading = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('*')).find(e => e.children.length===0 && e.textContent.trim()==='Research Notes');
  const container = heading.closest('li') || heading.parentElement.parentElement.parentElement;
  const editBtn = Array.from(container.querySelectorAll('button')).find(b => b.textContent.trim()==='Edit');
  editBtn.click();
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 900));
  const ta = document.querySelector('textarea');
  let val = ta.value;
  for (const [oldStr, newStr] of replacements) { val = val.split(oldStr).join(newStr); }
  setVal(ta, val);
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 300));
  const saveBtn = Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('button')).find(b => b.textContent.trim()==='Save');
  saveBtn.click();
  await new Promise(r => setTimeout(r, 800));
  return val; // returned value lets you verify the fix landed without a separate get_page_text call
}
```
**Note for all NEW facts going forward**: write Robert Stanley Babcock / Sandra Kay Babcock Walsberg / Bernadine Brand Babcock in full the first time on every new card — don't reintroduce the informal shorthand.
**Deliberately OUT of scope for this pass** (flagged for a separate future pass, not the immediate research-burst scope): the Blackwell/Brand line (Ann Blackwell, Marietta Blackwell, Jacob Brand, David Brand, Stella Schlottach) and the deep colonial Rhode Island ancestors already covered by the "From Westerly" paper — Robert's instruction was specifically about the Babcock research-burst people from the last two days.
**Technique note**: full-page `read_page(filter:"interactive")` right after selecting "Custom Event" returns ALL the dialog fields (label/date/location/description/submit) in one call — faster than separate `find` calls per field. Occasional `get_page_text` false negatives happen if checked too fast after JS-submit (race condition) — a screenshot or re-check confirms the save actually landed; don't assume failure without double-checking.
**Next step after all documents are attached**: Phase 3 per Robert's 2026-09-08 instruction — deep primary-source document research for all cards (census, vital records, marriage records) to substantiate everything added this research burst.

## ⭐⭐⭐⭐⭐ ANN BLACKWELL'S GRAVE FOUND (2026-09-09, St. Agnes Cemetery of Utica reply) — 120 YEARS UNMARKED, NOW LOCATED
St. Agnes Cemetery of Utica (Anne, stagnescemeteryutica@gmail.com) replied to the 2026-09-02 research request (thread 1a06488931976b66) at 10:27 AM 2026-09-09 with a burial record: **"Annie Blackwell," died 31 Oct 1905 of kidney disease, buried 1 Nov 1905, in the cemetery's single-grave section: Map 3, Section 48, Tier 11, Grave 459.** Record says she died in Rome, NY (matches the 1905 death notice already on file). **Parents listed as "Patrick and Catherine" — no last name given.** Anne's own caveat: these could be Ann's actual parents' first names, OR could be the names of an employer she was recorded under (unclear from the record itself; do not treat as confirmed parents without further work — but this is the first-ever lead on her parents' names, work it hard). The cemetery superintendent physically checked the plot and found **no headstone** — consistent with a woman buried at charitable expense in 1905, unmarked for over 120 years. A cemetery map PDF ("Map for Historical Tour 2.pdf," saved to Downloads) was attached showing the single-grave section as the large rectangle just below "MOHAWK STREET" — useful for Robert's planned physical visit.
**Why this matters:** this is the single biggest win of the whole Blackwell research thread — a specific, physical, locatable grave for a woman who had been essentially erased from the record for well over a century. It also hands us the first candidate names for Ann's own parents (Patrick & Catherine [Blackwell?]), a totally new research thread back toward Ireland.
**Next steps:** (1) attach this burial record (with the caveat about Patrick/Catherine) to Ann Blackwell's Ancestry profile (pid 252810842760) as a confirmed Burial fact; (2) treat "Patrick and Catherine" as an UNCONFIRMED lead, not settled parentage — cross-check against the 1905 death certificate #46049 once it arrives (that form has separate father-name/mother-maiden-name fields and is the authoritative source to confirm or refute this) and against Ireland parish records once a home parish is identified; (3) Robert wants to physically visit the grave — Map 3/Section 48/Tier 11/Grave 459, per the attached cemetery map; (4) since there's no headstone, worth discussing with Robert whether he'd want to arrange for one now that the exact plot is known.

  - The existing 6-sibling collateral branches (Lillie's/Gertrude's/Altha's/Hershel's/Clarence's/Rawleigh's) carried over unchanged from v1.
- **Design/technique notes for future charts**: use `stroke="currentColor"` (not raw `var()`) on inline SVG connector lines, paired with a CSS `color: var(--token)` on the wrapping element — raw CSS custom properties in SVG presentation attributes aren't reliably themed. The Browser pane's `computer` screenshot tool cannot act on local `file://` tabs and the sandboxed pane has no authenticated claude.ai session for a published Artifact URL — so verification of a local HTML chart before rendering is a manual code read, not a live screenshot; the actual visual check happens on the rendered PNG via the `Read` tool.

## 🔥 KIMBLE FAMILY HISTORY — 1881 Stockton, Utah house fire that killed 5 of Mary Louise Kimble Chapman's siblings (found 2026-09-11)
Robert uploaded a PDF titled "Kimble" to Google Drive — a family-history writeup on **John Alexander Kimble** and **Lucy Coleman Moores**, the parents of **Mary Louise Kimble**, who is already in our tree as the wife of **Fred(erick) Chapman Sr.** (the Chapman line documented in "The Chapman Family Album" findings). Downloaded, read in full, and saved to the repo at `Research Images and Documents/Kimble Family - John Alexander Kimble and Lucy Coleman Moores (1881 Stockton UT fire).pdf`.

**The story:** On August 3, 1881, a kerosene lamp exploded in the Kimble family's cabin in Stockton, Tooele County, Utah, and burned five of John & Lucy's children to death before their parents could get them out: Franklin Sylvester Kimble (10), Flora May Kimble (7), Elizabeth Ann Kimble (4), and infant twin boys Albert C. and Earnest A. Kimble (about 7 weeks old). All five are buried together in Stockton Cemetery under a marker (now broken/partly illegible) reading "In Memory of Frank, Flora, Lizzie and two Babies, children of Lucy and John KIMBLE." Lucy could not go on living there afterward and returned to her daughter (Mary Louise, i.e. the Chapman line) in Kansas; John continued on to California with their two remaining children.

**John Alexander Kimble** — b. 12 Aug 1833, New York State; raised from age 2 in Kirtland, Ohio (recalled seeing Mormon pioneers pass through); m. **Lucy Coleman Moores** 5 Jan 1856, New York; blacksmith by trade, later a windmill manufacturer (Kimble & Greene, Traver, CA) and rancher; d. 18 Apr 1924, Dinuba, Tulare Co., CA (obituary quoted in full in the source).

**Lucy Coleman Moores** — b. 5 Oct 1840, Westfield, Chautauqua Co., NY, daughter of Samuel Stillman Moores and Mary Colman; d. 18 Oct 1920, Dinuba, Tulare Co., CA.

**All 13 children of John & Lucy** (family moved MN → VA → WI → IL → KS → UT → CA as John chased blacksmithing/ranching work):
1. Fanny Caroline Kimble (b. 23 Aug 1857) — m. Francis Rudolph Hoyer, 10 Sep 1875, Glasco, Cloud Co., KS. Several of her own children also died young (Edward Rudolph, Bennie F., Ora M. — all infant deaths). Fanny d. 30 Jun 1895, Kansas, buried Glasco Cemetery.
2. **Mary Louise Kimble** (b. 21 Nov 1858) — m. **Frederick Chapman**, a farmer — **source gives TWO CONFLICTING marriage dates: "September 10, 1875" (first mention) vs. "December 21, 1874" (second mention), both "in Kansas" — UNCONFIRMED which is correct, flagged rather than picked.** 11 children, all born Glasco, Cloud Co., KS (only 6 named in this source: William Henry b. 24 Jan 1876 d. 24 Jan 1890; Stella Christina b. 25 Dec 1877; Edward Franklin b. 17 Aug 1881; Fred b. 8 Sep 1884; John Arthur b. 20 Dec 1886; Daniel b. 21 Jul 1889). Widowed 1915; moved to Dinuba/Tulare Co., CA; d. 30 May 1948, Dinuba.
3. Ella M. Kimble (b. 22 May 1860, Fairfax, VA) — never married; d. 10 Jul 1910, Tulare Co., CA.
4. John Edward Kimble (b. 12 Mar 1862, WI) — d. 30 Jul 1874, Glasco, KS, age 12, **killed by a rattlesnake bite**.
5. William Leonard Kimble (b. 5 Mar 1865, Iowa City, Wright Co., IA) — m. Maria Jane Pangborn, 9 Nov 1892, Cottage Grove, OR; mechanic; d. 15 Apr 1950, Bend, Deschutes Co., OR, buried Taylor Lane Cemetery.
6. Lucy Voadicia Kimble (b. 5 Jan 1867, Crystal Lake, McHenry Co., IL) — d. 20 Feb 1867 (infant), buried Lake Avenue Cemetery, Crystal Lake.
7. An unnamed son (b. 3 Mar 1869, Crystal Lake, IL) — d. 19 Mar 1869 (infant), buried Lake Avenue Cemetery, Crystal Lake.
8. **Franklin Sylvester Kimble** (b. 5 Aug 1871, KS) — d. 3 Aug 1881, Stockton, UT (**house fire**).
9. **Flora May Kimble** (b. 17 May 1874 per one passage / "age 7" in 1881 per the other — internally inconsistent birth year, UNCONFIRMED, KS) — d. 3 Aug 1881, Stockton, UT (**house fire**).
10. **Elizabeth Ann Kimble** (b. 28 Apr 1877, Stockton, Tooele Co., UT) — d. 3 Aug 1881 (**house fire**).
11. **Albert C. Kimble** (twin, b. 13 Jun 1881, Stockton, UT) — d. 3 Aug 1881, ~7 weeks old (**house fire**).
12. **Earnest A. Kimble** (twin, b. 13 Jun 1881, Stockton, UT) — d. 3 Aug 1881, ~7 weeks old (**house fire**).
13. Frank Alexander Kimble (b. 21 Aug 1882, Glasco Co. [sic, likely Cloud Co.], KS) — m. Julia Adelaide Voss (herself b. Tooele, UT, to Warren Voss & Janet A. Smith; previously married a man named Fry, 3 children from that marriage: Kyle, Bernice, Melvin Fry), 11 Mar 1893, Fresno, CA; d. 14 Jan 1953, San Joaquin Co., CA.

**Source-quality notes (flag, don't silently fix):**
- The two conflicting Mary Louise/Frederick Chapman marriage dates above (10 Sep 1875 vs. 21 Dec 1874) — the 10 Sep 1875 date is suspicious because the source separately assigns that *exact same date* to Fanny Caroline Kimble's wedding to Francis Hoyer, suggesting a transcription mix-up in the original document between two sisters' wedding dates. Needs an independent record to resolve.
- Flora May Kimble's birth year is given twice, inconsistently (implied 1874 by age math elsewhere in the piece vs. a implied ~1871 "age 7" at time of the 1881 fire the way the opening paragraph groups her with Franklin "both in Kansas" born "5 Aug 1871" — the opening paragraph's phrasing is itself ambiguous about whether that birth date belongs to both children or just Franklin).
- Original source has a few obvious name-spelling slips (John's wife's maiden name given as both "Moores" and "Moorse"; "Coleman" vs "Colman").

**Not yet done:** these 13 children/2 parents are not yet added as person records on Ancestry, and Mary Louise Kimble's existing Ancestry card has not yet had its Research Notes updated with this parentage/sibling/tragedy information. Natural next step if Robert wants it.

(twins), and Frank Alexander. Each new person also got their own "Research Notes" Custom Event fact citing the PDF; the 5 fire victims' notes cross-reference each other and their father's page.
- **John Kimball Sr's Research Notes rewritten** to present the fire story as TWO EXPLICITLY CONFLICTING ACCOUNTS side by side (the older, vaguer "Chapman Family Album" version — "five sons," Lucy blamed/hitchhiked pregnant, Frank A. "d.1895 age13" — vs. the new PDF's fuller, dated, named version) rather than silently picking one. Flagged specifically: the old note's claim Frank A. died in 1895 is contradicted by the new source's detailed record of his adult life through 1953; the old note's "pregnant when she left" detail doesn't fit the new source's own dates (Frank A. wasn't born until 13 months after the fire).

The "Add fact" flow's submit button is labeled **"Add"**, not "Save" (that's only on the "Edit fact" flow) — look for it scoped to the modal, since the page has multiple buttons with that exact text.
- **Compressed the 3 oversized source scans** that couldn't fit GitHub's 100MB-per-file limit, via PyMuPDF (extract each page's embedded image → downsample to ~950-1100px width, grayscale → re-encode JPEG quality ~52-55 → rebuild PDF): `1930AB.pdf` 223MB→58MB (206pp, guessed title "1930 Census Index (A-B surnames)" from filename — not yet confirmed what index/location this actually covers, worth double-checking next time it's opened), and two runs of 4-page-per-file newspaper scans merged into single volumes and compressed — `sr23_*.pdf` (45 files, 257MB→72MB) and `sr10_*.pdf` (10 files, 39MB→15MB), both guessed as **"Sabbath Recorder"** (a Seventh Day Baptist newspaper) volumes 23 and 10 based on OCR text visible in one sample page ("VOL XXIII NO 7... FEBRUARY 14, 1867") — plausible given the Babcock family's SDB background, but not independently confirmed against the actual masthead/cover page. All 3 compressed files pushed to the repo under `Source Texts (digitized books and records)/`.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 20 (2026-09-11, continued) — MAJOR FIND: a house fire killed 5 Kimble children, Stockton, Utah, 1881

While sweeping John Alexander Kimble + Lucy Coleman Moore's other children for unreviewed hints (continuing from Franklin Sylvester and Albert C./Earnest A. found last session), a pattern of same-day child deaths led to a genuinely major discovery -- previously completely undocumented anywhere in this research.

**The tragedy, from a contemporary newspaper account** (Deseret News, Salt Lake City, Sat. 27 Aug 1881, p.2, headline "FRIGHTFUL CALAMITY," found via newspapers.com):

> "A DISPATCH from Stockton, just received, states that five children and the house of John A. Kimble, a soldier, five miles up the canyon, were burned last evening, through the explosion of a lamp, which was blown over by a gale of wind, and set fire to five gallons of coal oil. The mother was a short distance away, leaving the house in charge of a boy 13 years old. The youngest were twin babes in the cradle. The roof fell in. The charred remains of the five children were found. [She] is nearly insane."

**On the evening of 26 August 1881**, the Kimble family's home five miles up a canyon from Stockton, Tooele County, Utah, caught fire when a kerosene lamp was knocked over by a windstorm, igniting five gallons of stored coal oil. Lucy (the mother) was away from the house at the time, having left the children in the care of a 13-year-old (likely an older sibling, not yet identified by name -- none of the known older children, per their own birth years, are confirmed to have been 13 in 1881; worth checking William Leonard or another son). The house burned to the ground with the roof collapsing, and **five children died**, identified by cross-referencing their own Ancestry Find A Grave / Family History Book hints:

- **Franklin Sylvester Kimball**, age 9 (b. 5 Aug 1871)
- **Flora May Kimball**, age 7 (b. 17 May 1874)
- **Elizabeth Ann Kimball**, age 4 (b. 28 Apr 1877)
- **Earnest A. Kimball**, twin, ~10 weeks old (b. 13 Jun 1881)
- **Albert C. Kimball**, twin, ~10 weeks old (b. 13 Jun 1881)

All five are buried at Stockton Town Cemetery, Stockton, Tooele County, Utah.

**Date discrepancy, flagged rather than silently resolved**: Ancestry's Find A Grave entries for 4 of the 5 children (Franklin, Elizabeth, Flora, and one Family History Book entry for the twins) give the death date as **3 August 1881**, while the newspaper -- a contemporary primary source, published the very next morning -- clearly places the fire on the evening of **26 August 1881** (matching a separate "Chapman Family History" tree entry for the twins, which also says 26 Aug). The newspaper account is almost certainly the more reliable date; the uniform "3 August" appearing across multiple Find A Grave memorials looks like an error that propagated from whoever first set them up, decades after the fact. Recorded here as CONFIRMED for the *event and cause* (house fire, lamp explosion, 5 children), UNCONFIRMED on the exact date pending a look at the children's actual headstones or a Tooele County death/probate record if one survives.

This tragedy explains, in one stroke, why 5 of John Alexander Kimble's children (of 13 total) have death dates clustered in 1881 -- something visible in the family's vital statistics for years but never previously connected to a specific event or cause. It is a genuinely significant, previously-lost piece of family history on Robert's own direct line (John Alexander Kimble -> Mary Louise Kimble -> Fredrick Wallace Chapman's wife -> daughter Mary Ella Chapman -> Rawleigh Oliver Babcock -> ... -> Robert). **Flagging this as a strong candidate for a dedicated narrative write-up**, in the style of "The Girl from the Poorhouse" or "Three Keowns in Blue," once there's time to pull the full clipping image, search for a follow-up article (funeral, inquest, or the 13-year-old's identity), and check whether the parents (John A. + Lucy) or the surviving siblings ever spoke of it.

**Not yet added to Ancestry** (write dialogs still down this session, confirmed unresponsive again on one profile at the start of this iteration) -- Research Notes citing this newspaper article are ready to add to all 5 children's profiles as soon as the UI cooperates, plus a Cause of Death fact ("house fire, Stockton, Tooele Co UT, 26 Aug 1881, per Deseret News 27 Aug 1881 p.2 'Frightful Calamity'") on each.

Committed and pushed. This is easily today's most significant find -- continuing the loop with lower priority items for the rest of this session, since this deserves to be the headline rather than getting buried under routine sweeps.

**Follow-up on the 1881 Kimble fire, same session**: Checked the surviving older/younger siblings for corroborating context.
- **William Leonard Kimball** (b. 5 Mar 1865) was 16 in Aug 1881, confirmed still in the Stockton, Utah household per the 1880 census (listed with sibling Franklin) -- the only known Kimble child close to the newspaper's "boy 13 years old" left in charge of the house. The 3-year age gap could easily be a telegraph-dispatch reporting error, or the age belonged to a hired hand/neighbor's son rather than a sibling -- not confirmed either way, but William Leonard remains the most plausible candidate given no other family member fits.
- **Frank Alexander Kimball**, born 21 Aug 1882 -- almost exactly one year after the fire -- in **Glasco, Cloud County, KANSAS**, not Utah. This confirms the family left Stockton, Utah and returned to Kansas in the immediate aftermath of losing five children there, presumably unable to stay at the site of the tragedy. He later settled in Dinuba, Tulare Co CA (m. Julia Adelaide Fry, d. 1953, buried Reedley, Fresno Co CA) -- joining the same California migration pattern as his Chapman/Babcock in-laws documented earlier this session.

This rounds out the full picture: John Alexander Kimble's family took his soldiering/homesteading path from Illinois (1860s) to Kansas (early 1870s) to Stockton, Utah (by 1880) -- where the fire happened in 1881 -- back to Kansas (1882, Frank Alexander's birth) and finally to California (Dinuba/Tulare area) by the early 1900s, where most of the surviving children and grandchildren (Chapman and Kimble lines alike) ended up living out their lives.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 21 (2026-09-11, continued) — a second, fuller account of the 1881 Kimble fire, and a genuine new puzzle

Found a SECOND, more detailed contemporary account of the fire from last session, in a different newspaper published the very next day:

**The Salt Lake Tribune, Sun. Aug 28 1881, p.1, "Special to Tribune," headline "Five Children Burned to Death":**
> "STOCKTON, Utah, August 27.—The residence of John A. Kimble, in **Soldier Canyon**, about five miles from here, was entirely consumed by fire about **10 o'clock last night**, caused by the explosion of a coal oil lamp. Five children, whose **ages ranged from two months to 13 years**, perished in the flames. It appears that Mrs. Kimble and the oldest son were in the cook house, about fifty feet from the residence, at the time of the accident, having left the children in charge of **a boy 13 years of age, who was rocking the cradle**. The wind blowing a perfect gale down the canyon..." [column cut off at the bottom of the page image; the rest of the report was not captured this session]

**New facts confirmed**: the canyon the family lived in was specifically named **Soldier Canyon** (fitting with "John A. Kimble, a soldier" from the Deseret News account -- possibly the canyon's name relates to his own or other soldiers' presence there, not yet established which way the naming went); the fire broke out around 10 PM on 26 Aug 1881; Lucy (the mother) and "the oldest son" were together in a separate cook house about 50 feet from the main house when the fire started, which is why they survived.

**A genuine unresolved puzzle, flagged rather than forced**: this account says the FIVE victims ranged in age from **two months to 13 years** -- but the four fire victims independently identified last session by name (Flora May, 7; Elizabeth Ann, 4; and infant twins Albert/Earnest, ~2 months) only reach a maximum age of 7, not 13. The fifth-named victim from last session, **Franklin Sylvester, was 9** -- also short of 13. Two readings are both plausible and neither can be confirmed from what's been found so far:
1. The "boy 13 years of age... rocking the cradle" was himself one of the five who died (trying to save the babies when the fire caught) -- meaning there is a genuinely unidentified 13-year-old Kimble child, and Franklin Sylvester's death, despite Find A Grave/Family History Book giving him the same year and general location, may NOT actually be connected to this fire at all (it's possible his death record is coincidentally similar, or was mis-dated/mis-connected by a modern researcher building his Find A Grave memorial).
2. The reporter's "13 years" upper bound is simply an error (early wire dispatches over telegraph were frequently imprecise on exact ages), and the real oldest victim was Franklin at 9 -- with the 13-year-old caretaker having survived (he's just the person "in charge," not counted among "the children" who died).

**Not resolved this session** -- would need either the actual death records/probate file from Tooele County, the children's headstones at Stockton Town Cemetery (if they survive and are legible), or a follow-up article (the funeral, likely covered by the same two papers within the following week) naming all five victims explicitly. Recording both readings here rather than picking one, per the standing "read the actual sources, don't force a conclusion" practice.

This is still clearly the same event as documented in session 20, just with a fuller, independently-corroborating (not merely repeating) account from a second paper -- and a legitimate new research question about exactly who all five victims were.

Committed and pushed.

**Quick spot-checks, same session**: Robert Harold Babcock and Ed Babcock (Clarence Babcock's sons, grandsons of Arthur Rueben Babcock) both already have their spouses/children fully recorded from an earlier session -- their hints only corroborate existing data (exact dates, WWII service records, obituaries). No new people. This branch (Clarence's descendants) appears already well-mined.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 22 (2026-09-11, continued) — the 1881 Kimble fire puzzle: RESOLVED

Went directly to Find A Grave (not just via Ancestry's hint-matching) and located Flora May Kimball's own memorial (findagrave.com/memorial/88858), which carries a detailed contributor biography (from member Marsha Murray) that settles the open question from last session:

> "This is one of 5 children killed in a house fire on 3 August 1881 and buried in the Cemetery. The children ranged from age 10 to twins almost 1 month. Parents John Alexander and Lucy Colemann Morres Kimball. **Franklin Sylvester age 10, Flora May age 7, Elizabeth Ann Age 4 and twin boys Albert C Kimble and Earnest A Kimble.**"

**This confirms the five victims are exactly the five already identified** (Franklin Sylvester, Flora May, Elizabeth Ann, and twins Albert C. and Earnest A.) -- no sixth, unidentified 13-year-old child. Franklin is given as "age 10" here (he was 9, five days short of his 10th birthday, by his own birth date of 5 Aug 1871 -- close enough to be an unsurprising rounding by family memory/a later contributor).

**Resolving the "13 years" reference from the contemporary newspapers**: the most sensible reading now is that the Salt Lake Tribune's "five children, whose ages ranged from two months to 13 years" was describing the age range of ALL the children in the household that night (i.e., including the 13-year-old survivor left "rocking the cradle"), not strictly the five who died. The 13-year-old was very likely a surviving older sibling -- possibly William Leonard Kimball (age 16 per his own birth date, a few years off but still the best fit among known siblings), or conceivably an unlisted/hired household member -- who was NOT among the five fatalities. This is a far more natural reading than presuming a 6th, otherwise-unattested child, and matches the burial register directly.

This closes out the investigative thread opened in sessions 20-21. The 1881 Kimble house-fire story can now be considered fully and consistently documented across three independent sources (2 contemporary newspapers + 1 Find A Grave contributor biography, itself apparently drawing on family records or a cemetery register), with only minor, immaterial discrepancies remaining (exact death date 3 Aug vs. the newspapers' clear 26 Aug fire date -- likely a burial-vs-death-date mixup somewhere in the chain, not worth chasing further; and "almost twins... 1 month" vs. the twins' own recorded birth date of 13 June 1881, which would make them closer to 7 weeks/~1.5 months old -- a minor rounding difference).

Committed and pushed. This closes the Kimble fire research thread -- it's ready for a dedicated narrative write-up whenever there's time, and for the Ancestry Research Notes/Cause of Death entries once the site's write dialogs recover.

**Note**: attempted to locate the Ewing/Ringle/Scholtz ancestor line (flagged as unswept) this session but couldn't find their pids efficiently -- Ancestry's tree-wide name search isn't reliably automatable (confirmed again this session; global ancestry.com search doesn't scope to a specific private tree, and the tree's own "Find in tree" box remains the known-unreliable typeahead documented in earlier sessions). Deferring this branch to a session where a pid can be found via a relationship hop from an already-known person, rather than guessing search URLs.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 46 (2026-09-11, continued) — found Ann Blackwell's actual obituary; new age/Utica-residence detail, but Irish county still not named

Took a fresh run at the long-stalled Ann Blackwell Irish-origin question (untouched since session 27) by searching Newspapers.com directly for her death notice rather than waiting for an Ancestry hint.

**Found and read her actual obituary**: *Daily Sentinel* (Rome, NY), Tuesday, **31 October 1905**, page 2 -- the day of her death:

> "Anna Blackwell. Anna Blackwell aged 52, died in this city at 7 a.m. today from a complication of diseases. The remains were taken to Utica, where she formerly resided, for interment."

Two new, genuine facts from this primary source:
- **Age at death: 52** -- implying a birth year of about **1852-1853**, a third distinct birth-year candidate alongside the "1846" and "1848" figures already circulating across different trees (sessions 30, 35). Obituary ages are sometimes approximate, so this doesn't resolve the birth-year question outright, but it's now three independent (and mutually inconsistent) estimates on record -- flagged, not adopted as final.
- **She formerly resided in Utica, NY (also Oneida Co, like Rome), and was buried there**, not in Rome. This is new geographic detail -- previous research focused on Rome NY records; a burial and prior residence in Utica opens a parallel line of record-searching (Utica city directories, Utica-area church/parish burial records, Utica cemetery records) not yet tried.

**Unfortunately, the obituary itself is very short and generic** -- no birthplace, no county of origin, no next-of-kin, no maiden name confirmation, nothing pointing toward Ireland at all. It does not advance the core Irish-county question.

**Checked Find A Grave for a Utica burial record**: no matching memorial found for "Ann/Anna Blackwell" who died 1905 in Utica or anywhere within +/-5 years under that name -- she currently has **no Find A Grave memorial at all**, so a headstone inscription (which sometimes names an Irish home county on period Catholic/immigrant headstones) can't be checked remotely. This would require either finding which Utica cemetery she's buried in through city burial permits/records, or a future in-person or local-genealogical-society lookup.

Committed and pushed. **Next concrete step for this thread**: search Utica (not just Rome) city directories and Catholic parish burial registers around 1905 for "Anna/Ann Blackwell," and/or search Utica-based newspapers (not just the Rome Daily Sentinel) for a fuller obituary that a bigger city paper might have run with more biographical detail.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 51 (2026-09-11, continued) — found and read the actual "Pioneer History of Cortland County" passage describing the Babcock brothers' 1799 frontier settlement

Followed up on session 50's lead: located the full 1859 original edition of ***Pioneer History; or, Cortland County and the Border Wars of New York*** by H.C. Goodwin on HathiTrust (public domain, full view -- https://babel.hathitrust.org/cgi/pt?id=loc.ark:/13960/t12n5c884), and used its in-text search to find the actual Babcock-family passage rather than just relying on the cropped photo hints.

**Page 215, found via in-text search for "Babcock"** -- an actual narrative account of the family's founding settlement:

> "During the year 1799, several settlements were made. Peleg Babcock, accompanied by his brothers Solomon and Asa Howard, came in from Leyden, Massachusetts, and selected locations. Peleg settled on the south part of lot 82. Solomon located on the north-west part of the same lot, while Howard stuck his post a little to the east of Solomon..."

This is a real, dateable, narrative primary-source description of **Peleg Babcock Jr. (1772-1827) and Solomon Babcock (1776-1867) arriving together from Leyden, Massachusetts in 1799 and founding adjoining homestead claims on lot 82** in what became Cortland County, NY -- consistent with and adding real texture to the family's already-documented migration (session 50). Note: this "Peleg" is the younger **Peleg Jr.**, Solomon's brother (both sons of the elder Peleg W. Babcock Sr., sessions 43/49/50) -- not their father, who remained in Rhode Island/Massachusetts.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 54 (2026-09-11/12, continued) — found Silas Rawson & Abigail Chapin's actual genealogy-book entry: both sets of their parents now known, plus a Bunker Hill soldier in the extended family

Followed up on session 53 by applying the HathiTrust technique (session 51) to **"The Chapin Book of Genealogical Data"** by Gilbert Warren Chapin (1924), a genuine 2-volume published Chapin family genealogy, full view on HathiTrust (https://babel.hathitrust.org/cgi/pt?id=mdp.39015071169299). An in-text search for "Rawson" (26 matches) found the exact entry, page 291:

> "ABIGAIL CHAPIN. dau. Joseph & Mary (Nelson) Chapin. b. May 10, 1732, Mendon, d. Conway, Mass. m. Jan. 5, 1762, Mendon, Silas Rawson, son of Nathaniel & Mary (Thwing) Rawson, b. Nov. 17, 1739, Mendon, d. Aug. 26, 1819, æ. 80, Palmyra, N.Y."

This resolves both sides of the previous "Potential parents" flag on Robert's tree at once:
- **Abigail Chapin's parents: Joseph Chapin and Mary Nelson.**
- **Silas Rawson's parents: Nathaniel Rawson and Mary (Thwing) Rawson** -- extending the confirmed Rawson line one more generation back (Nathaniel & Mary Thwing Rawson -> Silas Rawson & Abigail Chapin -> Rev. Daniel Rawson (session 53) -> Anson George Rawson (session 52, War of 1812 veteran) -> Robert Ira Rawson (1829-1901) -> the already fully-documented later generations).
- **Silas Rawson's death is specifically located**: Palmyra, New York, 26 Aug 1819, age 80.

**A collateral Revolutionary War soldier noted**: the page's text immediately above this entry, describing a "Samuel [Chapin]" (likely one of Abigail's brothers, based on the family numbering), states: **"Mr. Chapin was a Revolutionary Soldier. Tradition says he was at the Battle of Bunker Hill."** This is not a direct-line ancestor (a sibling of Abigail, not Abigail herself), so not adopted into the confirmed-Patriot count, but worth noting as further evidence of how deeply this extended family was involved in the Revolutionary War, alongside the already-confirmed Seth Strong and Oliver Babcock Jr.

Not yet added to Ancestry (write outage ongoing) -- ready-to-enter backlog: Silas Rawson's and Abigail Chapin's now-confirmed parents, and Silas's specific death location/age.

## 🌙 AUTONOMOUS SESSION 67 (2026-09-12, continued) — James Chapman obituary finally read in full: rich immigration narrative, a death-date correction, and new unidentified children

**Solved the session 63 technical blocker.** Ancestry's in-page media-viewer "Zoom in" control kept navigating away instead of magnifying (sessions 63-64). This session, instead of using that control, navigated directly to the media item's own URL (extracted via the hint's underlying href rather than clicking it) — `mediaui-viewer/tree/86228220/person/142084019084/media/5c3fd710-...` — which loaded cleanly. That viewer page links to the item's original source: "Heimweh23 originally shared this article... from Newspapers.com." Following that link straight to Newspapers.com's own article page, `get_page_text` pulled the full, clean OCR transcription in one shot — no zooming needed at all. **Technique for future sessions**: when an Ancestry media item is a Newspapers.com clipping, always check for a "shared this article from Newspapers.com" link in the viewer's right-hand panel and follow it to Newspapers.com's own article page rather than fighting Ancestry's in-page viewer.

**Full obituary text** (The Sun, Glasco KS, published shortly after 17 Oct 1908):

> Obituary of James Chapman. James Chapman was born in Norfolk county, England, on February 14, 1830, where he grew to manhood. He died at the home of his son, James Chapman, in Glasco, Friday morning, October 16, 1908 at about 5:30 o'clock, aged 78 years, 8 months and two days... Mr. Chapman was married to Miss Emma Harris in 1851. To this union were born eight children, three of whom died in infancy. Five survive him, also a large number of grand-children and great grand-children. In 1867 he came from England to the United States with his family, and settled in Illinois. From there he came to Kansas in 1870, and homesteaded [a] farm six miles southwest of Glasco. Brother Chapman united with the M.E. church a number of years ago. His wife preceded him in death, and was also buried from this church... Funeral services were conducted in the M.E. church by Eld. Edward L. Pettus, assisted by Rev. Bates and Eld. C.T. Faris, on Saturday, October 17. Interment was made in the Glasco cemetery.

**New/corrected facts**:
1. **Death date correction**: the obituary explicitly and consistently states death on **"Friday morning, October 16, 1908"** with funeral the very next day, **"Saturday, October 17."** Checked the calendar: 16 Oct 1908 was in fact a Friday (confirmed by day-count from 1 Jan 1908, a Wednesday), and 17 Oct 1908 a Saturday — fully internally self-consistent. Robert's tree currently shows death as **23 Oct 1908** (exactly one week later, also a Friday) — likely an indexing artifact where a database pulled the newspaper's publish/microfilm date rather than the date actually stated in the obituary text. **Recommend correcting the tree's death date to 16 Oct 1908** once the write UI recovers, citing this obituary directly as the source.
2. **A previously unknown son, also named James Chapman**, at whose Glasco home the elder James died — likely "James Chapman Jr.," not yet a documented child in the tree. Worth a targeted search once the write UI recovers.
3. **Family size clarified**: 8 children total from the marriage to Emma Harris, **3 died in infancy** (unnamed, unidentified), **5 survived him**. Robert's tree currently documents only 3 children (Fredrick Wallace, Walter John, Emma Caroline) plus this newly-found son James Jr. = 4 identified survivors, meaning **at least 1 more surviving adult child, plus all 3 infant deaths, remain unidentified** — a good lead for a future session (Kansas/Illinois census records circa 1870-1880 in the Chapman household would likely name them).
4. **Emigration timeline confirmed with precision**: emigrated from England to the United States in **1867**, first settling in **Illinois**; moved to **Kansas in 1870**, homesteading a farm six miles southwest of Glasco. This is exactly the kind of immigration narrative detail Robert's original research directive asked for, and matches/refines the "5th immigrant-origin region" finding from session 62 (Norfolk, England).
5. **Religious/community detail**: the family were Methodist Episcopal (M.E.) church members in Glasco; his wife Emma (Harris) Chapman was buried from the same church; funeral conducted by three named clergy (Eld. Edward L. Pettus, Rev. Bates, Eld. C.T. Faris) -- minor color for a future narrative write-up, not independently significant genealogically.

Not yet changed in Ancestry (write outage ongoing) -- ready-to-enter backlog: correct James Chapman's death date to 16 Oct 1908 (cite this obituary); add a new son "James Chapman Jr." once located as a proper record; flag the 3 infant deaths and 1+ unidentified surviving child as an open research lead for Illinois (1867-1870) and Kansas (1870+) census/vital records.

**Session assessment**: a genuine win after 3 sessions of technical friction (63, 64 touched on this without progress) -- both the specific obituary-reading blocker AND a real substantive discovery (a death-date correction plus new unidentified children) came out of finally getting a clean read. Exactly the kind of "make it worth the subscription" deep-newspapers.com research the original directive asked for.

Committed and pushed.

Follow-up to sessions 67-68's James Chapman obituary/1880-census work, trying to find the one still-unidentified surviving child (of 5 named in the obituary) and the 3 infant deaths.

- **1900 census reviewed directly** (Solomon, Cloud Co., Kansas): by 1900, James Chapman (age 69, widowed -- confirms wife Emma Harris's 1898 death, already known) was living as "Father" in the household of his son **Walter John Chapman**, recorded here as "**W J Chapman**" (age 27, matching "John W." age 7 in the 1880 census -- confirms this is the same person, consistent with session 68). Walter John's own household: wife Annie (21), daughter Edith (2), son Earle (1), plus a boarder Jesse Jones. No additional Chapman children appear in this record.

**Conclusion**: the missing 5th surviving child and the 3 infant deaths remain unidentified after a genuine search attempt. Best remaining approach for a future session: Illinois county-level birth/death registers (rather than federal census, which appears to have missed the family's short Illinois stay) for the 1867-1870 window, or a wider net on Kansas death records/Find A Grave for any Chapman infant burials near Glasco.

Munsell)** on HathiTrust -- the original public-domain edition, full view (mdp.39015010461716, 888 page scans, 2 vols; a 1975 reprint also exists but is search-only). This is exactly the right family/place match for Robert's confirmed Revolutionary War Patriot **Deacon Seth Strong** (b. ~1719, Northampton MA, m. Lois [surname unknown] 1740, d. 1799 -- found session 38).

Searching "Seth Strong" in this book returns 16 exact-phrase hits, confirming the name recurs across MULTIPLE generations/branches of this large family (a Seth Strong of Woodbury CT, one of Durham CT with wife Phebe, one born 1761 who was "deaf and dumb," etc.) -- "Seth Strong" was evidently a common recurring given name in this family, similar to how "James" repeats in the Chapman line. Checked several of the specific page hits directly (pages ~485, 575-577, 250-252) and none matched Robert's specific Seth Strong (wife Lois, married 1740, died 1799) on the details visible.

**Not resolved this session**: which (if any) of the 16 "Seth Strong" entries in this book is Robert's own ancestor. The book's own internal pagination has confusing seq-vs-printed-page offsets that shift partway through (plates/blank pages inserted), which cost significant time this session; direct URL navigation to a guessed sequence number was unreliable, while using the "Search in This Text" panel's own result links worked correctly. The search text field also proved fragile to keyboard-shortcut editing (Ctrl+A sometimes selected the whole page instead of the field) -- future attempts should click precisely into the field and verify the query in the URL (`q1=...`) before assuming a search ran cleanly.

**Recommended for a future session**: retry the search with more specific terms less likely to collide with other generations -- e.g. "Lois Strong" (his wife's given name, fairly distinctive combined with "Strong"), or search for the specific detail "Major Leavenworth" (his Revolutionary War company commander, from session 38's find) which is far less likely to produce false-generation matches than "Seth Strong" alone.
